\displaystyle\,-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left[\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\rvert^{2}+a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]-4\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(6.4a)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}

\displaystyle\,\left(-2a(N+1)\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}+1}\phi-2a\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\nabla\phi\rangle_{G}+6C\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\right)\cdot(\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi)

(6.4b)

\displaystyle\,-2a(N+1)\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rangle_{G}-2Ca\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rangle_{G}

(6.4c)

\displaystyle\,-2\left(\mathfrak{P}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{P}_{L,Junk}\right)\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi-2a^{4}\langle\mathfrak{Q}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{Q}_{L,Junk},\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rangle_{G}

(6.4d)

\displaystyle\,+2(N+1)a\cdot(\Sigma^{\sharp})^{ij}\nabla_{i}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\nabla_{j}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi-2N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}

(6.4e)

\displaystyle\,-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left[\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\rvert^{2}+a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]-4\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

Note that, for
L=0
, the equivalent equality holds where the borderline and junk terms are replaced by
-2a^{4}\Psi\langle\nabla N,\nabla\phi\rangle_{G}
(to verify this, insert (

) and (

) instead of (

) and (

)). We now go through (

)-(

) term by term:

After integrating by parts, the first term in (

) reads

(6.5)

\int_{M}2a(N+1)\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\rangle_{G}+2a\langle\nabla N,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rangle_{G}\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,.

The first term
precisely
cancels the first term in (
6.4b
), while we can use the bootstrap assumption (

) to estimate the other term in (

) up to constant by

\varepsilon a^{3-c\sigma}\cdot a^{2}\|\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\|\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{3-c\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)\,.

For the second term in (

), we use (

) to estimate
\nabla\phi
and Corollary
5.5
at order
L
to deal with the lapse, getting

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}2a\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\nabla\phi\rangle_{G}\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-1}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{3}a^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{2}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{2}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{2}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-3)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}_{\text{not present for }L=2}\,.

Repeating this argument for
L=0
, the last two lines do not appear.

To deal with the remaining term in (
6.4a
), we can insert the following zero on the right hand side of the differential equality, where the equality (
6.6
) holds due to (

):

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{3}\int_{M}\text{{div}}_{G}\left(\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{3}\int_{M}\Delta^{{\frac{L}{2}}+1}N\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N+\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(6.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{3}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}^{2}-\frac{3}{8\pi}\left(\frac{1}{3}\dot{a}a^{3}+12\pi C^{2}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\right)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-6C\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{3}\left[\mathfrak{N}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{N}_{L,Junk}\right]\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

Note that the first line has a negative sign, so (after absorbing a few terms into it without changing the sign, see namely lapse quantities in (
6.7
) and (
6.8a
)), we pull it to the left hand side of the differential equality. Further, the first term in the second line of (
6.6
) precisely cancels the third term in (
6.4a
). That leaves the borderline and junk terms in (

), for which we use (

) and (

) (along with
\dot{a}\simeq a^{-2}
due to (

)) to get for

:

\displaystyle\,\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{3}\left\lvert\int_{M}\left[\mathfrak{N}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{N}_{L,Junk}\right]\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle+\,\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(N,\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{3}a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\varepsilon^{3}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-3)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}_{\text{not present for }L=2}

Again, the same estimate holds for
L=0
with the last two lines dropped.

From (
6.4a
)-(
6.4b
), only the term
-2Ca\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rangle_{G}
still needs to be handled: Using the inequality (

) arising from the Friedman equation, we can estimate this by

(6.7)

\int_{M}2\sqrt{\frac{3}{4\pi}}\dot{a}a^{3}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\leq\int_{M}4\dot{a}a^{3}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}+\frac{3}{16\pi}{\dot{a}}a^{3}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,.

Note that the first term precisely cancels the final term in (
6.4e
), while the second term can be absorbed into the first term in (
6.6
) while preserving that term’s sign.

To bound the error terms in (
6.4c
), we insert the the borderline term estimates (
11.17d
) and (
11.17e
) as well as the junk term estimates (
11.19f
) and (

), where (

) is used to estimate odd order by even order energies where needed. Furthermore, observe that we can estimate the
\mathfrak{Q}_{L}
-terms as

\left(a^{2}\|\mathfrak{Q}_{L}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)},

so all borderline and junk terms arising from it, beside the scalar field energies, are dominated by terms occurring elsewhere.

Finally, all terms that remain, namely (
6.4d
) and the first term in (

), can be bounded by
\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)
due to the strong base level estimate (
4.2b
) and (

). In summary, and always only keeping the worst terms for each energy and squared norm, this yields for
L\geq 4
:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\dot{a}a^{3}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)

(6.8a)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-3}+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon a^{-3}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)\right)

(6.8b)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)

(6.8c)

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\left[\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(N,\cdot)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(6.8d)

\displaystyle\,\underbrace{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\text{not present for }L=2}

The lapse energies in (
6.8a
) can now also be absorbed into those on the left hand side of the inequality by updating the implicit constant in
“
\lesssim
”
. We can treat the lower order lapse energies in (

) with Corollary
5.5
and see that the resulting terms are all dominated by terms we already have on the right hand side of the inequality above.

Inserting these estimates and integrating over
(t,t_{0}]
then yields (
6.3
) for
L\geq 4
, and the statement for
L=2
is obtained completely analogously.

As mentioned earlier, (

) is replaced by the following term for

:

\int_{M}-2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi\langle\nabla N,\nabla\phi\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-3}\int_{M}a^{2}\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{G}\cdot a^{2}\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\lesssim\varepsilon\dot{a}a^{3}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(N,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)\,,

Here, we applied (

) and (

). Both of these terms can be absorbed into terms that are already present, and (

) then follows by dealing with terms in
\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)
as described and integrating.
∎

To close the argument, we will need a scaled scalar field energy estimate at the odd orders
L+1
, which is not covered by the previous lemma and we hence establish separately:

Lemma 6.4

(Odd order scalar field energy estimate)
.

For
L\in 2\mathbb{N}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\leq L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we have:

(6.9)

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,t)+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left\{\dot{a}(s)a(s)^{7}\mathcal{E}^{(L+2)}(N,s)+\dot{a}(s)a(s)^{3}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,s)\right\}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t_{0})^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\Sigma,s)+\left(\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,s)+\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,s)\right.

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)+\left(\varepsilon^{3}a^{-3}+\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+{\varepsilon}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)\right)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}\underbrace{\left.+\left(\varepsilon^{3}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{2}a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\right\}\,ds}_{\text{not present for }L=2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

At order
1
, the analogous estimate holds where the last three lines of (
6.9
) are dropped.

Proof.

These estimates follow completely analogously to Lemma
6.2
, with the exception that high order lapse terms can now be estimated at order
L+2
due to the the scalar field energy being scaled by
a^{4}
. In particular, we note that to deal with the analogous term to (
6.4a
), one now inserts the following zero on the right and applies the commuted lapse equation (
2.37b
):

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{7}\int_{M}\text{{div}}_{G}\left(\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\cdot\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}+1}N\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left\{-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{7}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}+1}N\rvert^{2}-\frac{3}{8\pi}\left(\frac{1}{3}\dot{a}a^{3}+12\pi C^{2}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\right)\cdot a^{4}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\,\,\phantom{\int_{M}}\left.-6C\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot a^{4}\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi\rangle-\frac{3}{8\pi}\dot{a}a^{7}\langle\mathfrak{N}_{L+1,Border}+\mathfrak{N}_{L+1,Junk},\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rangle_{G}\right\}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

For
L=0
, the argument is again the same as at higher orders with less complicated junk terms. We briefly highlight some specific junk terms: The term analogous to (
6.4c
) is now estimated as follows using (
4.13a
):

\displaystyle a^{4}\cdot\int_{M}-2a\langle\nabla\Psi\nabla N,\nabla^{2}\phi\rangle_{G}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\int_{M}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{G}\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{4}\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\dot{a}a^{3}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(N,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 1)}(\phi,\cdot)

Further, note that, by the commutator formula (
11.8c
) and applying (
4.4e
), one has

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}a^{8}[\partial_{t},\Delta]\phi\cdot\Delta\phi\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{5-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\nabla N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right)\|\Delta\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\phi,\cdot)+a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+\varepsilon a^{6-c\sigma}\cdot\dot{a}a^{3}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(N,\cdot)\,.

∎

6.1.2.
Sobolev norm estimate for
\nabla\phi

To improve the bootstrap assumptions on
\nabla\phi
, we will need sharper bounds than those on
a^{4}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{L}}^{2}
that will follow from bounds on
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)
:

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
l\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Then, for
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
l\leq 17
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim(1+\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}})\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l+1}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}+\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\Psi\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}

Proof.

By (
4.2a
),
\Psi+C>\frac{C}{2}
holds if
\varepsilon
is chosen small enough. Consequently, we can rearrange (
2.29b
) and apply the product rule to obtain

\lvert\nabla^{l}\nabla\phi\rvert_{G}=\frac{1}{8\pi}\left\lvert\nabla^{l}\left(\frac{\text{{div}}_{G}\Sigma}{\Psi+C}\right)\right\rvert_{G}\lesssim\sum_{I_{\Sigma}+I_{\Psi}=l}\lvert\nabla^{I_{\Sigma}+1}\Sigma\rvert_{G}\lvert\nabla^{I_{\Psi}}\Psi\rvert_{G}\,.

The statement then follows by integrating over
\Sigma_{t}
and applying (
4.2a
) and (
4.2b
).
∎

6.2.
Energy estimates for the Bel-Robinson variables

In this subsection, we collect the energy estimates for the Bel-Robinson variables:

Lemma 6.6

(Bel-Robinson energy estimates)
.

Let
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Then one has

(6.10)

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,t)+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\int_{M}\left[8\pi C^{2}a(s)^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Sigma,\bm{E}\rangle_{G}+6\frac{\dot{a}(s)}{a(s)}(N+1)\lvert\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left({\varepsilon}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,s)+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\phi,s)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,ds

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,t_{0})}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,s)+\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,s)\,ds

as well as, for
L\in 2\mathbb{N},\,2\leq L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

(6.11)

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,t)+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\int_{M}\left[8\pi C^{2}a(s)^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}+6\frac{\dot{a}(s)}{a(s)}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,ds

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,s)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,s)\right.

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{31}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)

\displaystyle\,\left.\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(W,s)\right)+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds}_{\text{not present for }L=2}\right\}\,.

Remark 6.7
.

We preemptively note that the error terms on the left hand side, once combined with the similar terms on the left hand side in Lemma
6.8
and given suitable weights, will turn out to have positive sign, even if they do not have definite sign in isolation.

The main idea in deriving this inequality is that we can use the algebraic identity (
11.3d
) and integration by parts to exploit the Maxwell system that lies at the core of the Bel-Robinson evolution equations. As a result, we avoid having higher order energies of the Bel-Robinson variables on the right hand side of the integral energy inequalities (which would break the bootstrap argument), then only having to deal with scalar field and Ricci energies at the next derivative level.

Proof.

We first prove (
6.11
), and then explain how the same ideas lead to the simpler estimate (
6.10
). To this end, we start out by taking the time derivative of the energy as usual:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left[\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]-2\left(\langle\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}+\langle\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2(\partial_{t}G^{-1})^{i_{1}j_{1}}(G^{-1})^{i_{2}j_{2}}\left[\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}_{j_{1}j_{2}}+\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\bm{E}
and
\bm{B}
are symmetric and tracefree, thus symmetrizations become redundant, and any scalar product with a tensor that is pure trace or with an antisymmetric tensor can be dropped.
10
10

10

Recall the superscript
“
\parallel
”
notation for error terms, see Remark
2.12
.
With this in hand, we get, inserting (
2.38a
) and (
2.38b
):

(6.12a)

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr\{\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(-6(N+1)+9N)\left(\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right)

(6.12b)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2(N+1)a^{-1}\left(\langle\text{{curl}}_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}-\langle\text{{curl}}_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right)

(6.12c)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2a^{-1}\left(\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\wedge_{G}\bm{B},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}-\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\wedge_{G}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(6.12d)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-8\pi C^{2}a^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}-8\pi a(\Psi+C)\langle\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\nabla\phi,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}

(6.12e)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-8\pi a(\Psi+C)(N+1)\langle\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}

(6.12f)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+16\pi a(N+1)\langle\nabla\phi\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Psi,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}

(6.12g)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+a^{3}\bm{\varepsilon}[G]\ast\nabla\phi\ast\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}

(6.12h)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(N+1)a^{-3}\Sigma\ast\left(\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}+\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\right)

(6.12i)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-2\langle\mathfrak{E}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{E}_{L,top}+\mathfrak{E}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}

(6.12j)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-2\langle\mathfrak{B}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{B}_{L,top}+\mathfrak{B}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}\biggr\}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

For (
6.12a
), we pull
6(N+1)\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}
to the left. This leaves

\int_{M}-6\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}+3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}+9N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G},

where we can estimate the last two terms up to constant by
\varepsilon a^{1-c\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)
by (
3.17h
) and can drop the first term since it is nonpositive.

Regarding (
6.12b
), note that we have

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-1}\left(\langle\text{{curl}}_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}-\right.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\left.\langle\text{{curl}}_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rangle_{G}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=-a^{-1}\text{{div}}_{G}\left(\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\wedge_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\right).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Hence, the absolute value of (
6.12b
), using (
11.4c
) for the wedge product and (
3.17h
), can be bounded by:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2a^{-1}(N+1)\text{{div}}_{G}\left(\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\wedge_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert=

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2a^{-1}\langle\nabla N,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\wedge_{G}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rangle_{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{M}a^{-1}\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{G}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{3-c\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)

For (
6.12c
), we use the pointwise wedge product estimate (
11.4d
) and a priori estimates (
4.2c
) and (
4.4g
) to bound it as follows:

\displaystyle\lvert\eqref{eq:en-eq-BR3}\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,2a^{-1}\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}\left(\lvert\bm{B}\rvert_{G}\cdot\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}+\lvert\bm{E}\rvert_{G}\cdot\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)\right)

We pull the first term of (
6.12d
) to the left as well, and estimate the second using the strong
C_{G}
-norm estimates (
4.2a
) and (
4.4e
) by

\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}\lesssim a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{-1}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)\,.

Moving on to (
6.12e
)-(
6.12g
), we see [using (
4.2a
), (
4.4e
), (
4.13a
) with
\zeta=\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi
and (
3.8
)]:

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lvert\eqref{eq:en-eq-BR5}\rvert\lesssim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\left(a^{-3}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)}+a^{-1}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)}+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+a^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lvert\eqref{eq:en-eq-BR6}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\eqref{eq:en-eq-BR7}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\phi,\cdot)}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)\right]

We can estimate (
6.12h
) by
\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)
as usual, and obtain the following in summary:

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+8\pi C^{2}a^{-3}\int_{M}(N+1)\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}+6\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\int_{M}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\varepsilon a^{-3}+a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+a^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+a^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon a^{-3}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(N,\cdot)+a^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,\left[\|\mathfrak{E}_{L,Border}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{E}_{L,top}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{E}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right.

\displaystyle\,\left.+\|\mathfrak{B}_{L,Border}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{B}_{L,top}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{B}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right]\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)}

We can now apply Corollary
5.5
for
2l=L
to estimate the lapse energy in the second line (leading to borderline scalar field energy and
\Sigma
-energy contributions as well as junk terms), and insert the borderline (see (
11.17k
)), top (see (
11.18a
) and (
11.18b
)) and junk estimates (see (
11.19n
)), dealing with the lapse energies there analogously. In particular, the top order curvature terms arise as follows:

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{E}_{M,top}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a^{-1}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{B}_{M,top}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3}\sqrt{a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(M)}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a^{-3}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

Hence, both top order curvature terms can be bounded by
\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a^{-3}\cdot a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(M-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
.

Integrating the inequality yields (
6.11
).

For (
6.10
), we get applying (
2.31a
) and (
2.31b
) and again using that
\bm{E}
and
\bm{B}
are symmetric and tracefree:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr\{\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(-6(N+1)+9N)\left(\lvert\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+2(N+1)\left(\langle\text{{curl}}\bm{E},\bm{B}\rangle_{G}-\langle\text{{curl}}\bm{B},\bm{E}\rangle_{G}\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+2\left(\langle\nabla N\wedge\bm{B},\bm{E}\rangle_{G}-\langle\nabla N\wedge\bm{E},\bm{B}\rangle_{G}\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(N+1)a^{-3}\Sigma\ast\left(\bm{E}\ast\bm{E}+\bm{B}\ast\bm{B}\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-8\pi a^{-3}(N+1)(\Psi+C)^{2}\langle\Sigma,\bm{E}\rangle_{G}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+\left[\dot{a}a^{3}\nabla\phi\ast\nabla\phi\ast\bm{E}+a(\Psi+C)\cdot\nabla N\ast\nabla\phi\right]\ast\bm{E}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+a(N+1)\left[\nabla\phi\ast\nabla\Psi+\Sigma\ast\nabla\phi\ast\nabla\phi+(\Psi+C)\nabla^{2}\phi\right]\ast\bm{E}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(N+1)\bm{\varepsilon}[G]\ast\left(a^{3}\nabla^{2}\phi\ast\nabla\phi+a^{-1}(\Psi+C)\Sigma\ast\nabla\phi\right)\ast\bm{B}\biggr\}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

The first two lines are treated as in the general case. For the third line, we get
\varepsilon a^{3-c\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)
with (
11.4d
) and (
3.17h
), while the fourth term is bounded by
\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)
with (
4.2b
). This leaves the surviving matter terms in the final four lines.

We pull
\int_{M}8\pi a^{-3}(N+1)C^{2}\langle\Sigma,\bm{E}\rangle_{G}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}
to the left as before. For the remaining terms, we can apply a priori estimates (
4.2a
), (
4.4a
) and (
4.4e
), the bootstrap assumption (
3.17h
) and Lemma
4.1
for
N
, which yields the following bound up to constant remaining terms in the last three lines:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)}\cdot

\displaystyle\left[a^{-1}\cdot a^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\varepsilon a^{-3}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}\right]

Applying (
4.13a
) to the scalar field norm and then (
3.8
), this leads to (
6.10
) along with the previous observations.
∎

6.3.
Energy estimates for the second fundamental form

For the energy estimates for
\Sigma
, we again first derive even order integral estimates:

Lemma 6.8

(Energy estimates for the second fundamental form for even orders)
.

Let
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Then, one has:

(6.13)

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,t)+2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\int_{M}\left[a(s)^{-3}(N+1)\langle\bm{E},\Sigma\rangle_{G}+\frac{\dot{a}(s)}{a(s)}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,s)\,ds+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,s)\,ds

For
L\in 2\mathbb{N},L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, the following holds:

(6.14)

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,t)+2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\int_{M}\left[a(s)^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,ds+\frac{\dot{a}(s)}{a(s)}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,ds

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\Bigr\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{5-c\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)+\varepsilon^{2}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon^{2}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{2}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)}_{\text{not present for }L=2}\Bigr\}\,ds

Remark 6.9
.

The main hurdle of dealing with the second fundamental form is that a high order curvature term occurs in the evolution equation. It is to precisely this end that the Bel-Robinson variables needed to be introduced, since (
2.36d
) is what facilitates controlling said term without having to use
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
or similar high order metric energies. Again, the resulting leading terms will turn out to have definite sign when combined with the Bel-Robinson energy estimates above.

Proof.

Here, we omit the proof for the inequality at order zero since is completely analogous in structure to the one for orders 2 and higher and the only differences that arise are that lower order error terms do not occur.

Once again, we start out by differentiating
-\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
and insert (
2.35
):

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-2\left\langle\partial_{t}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\right\rangle_{G}+(\partial_{t}G^{-1})\ast G^{-1}\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left\{2a\langle\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N,\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}-2a(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\text{{Ric}}[G],\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}+\right.

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(\partial_{t}G^{-1})\ast G^{-1}\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}\left.-2\langle\mathfrak{S}_{L,Border},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}-2\langle\mathfrak{S}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\right\}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

For the first term, one can use (
4.13a
), Corollary
5.5
at order
L
and (
3.8
) to bound its absolute value by the following:

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a\|\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\|_{\dot{H}^{2}_{G}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left[a^{-3}\sqrt{a^{8}\mathcal{E}^{(L+2)}(N,\cdot)}+a^{1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)}\right]\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\biggr[\varepsilon a^{-3}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}+a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{\left(\varepsilon^{2}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon a^{1-c\sigma}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-3)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}}_{\text{not present for }L=2}\biggr]\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}+a^{1-c\sigma}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{31}{8}}a^{-3}+\varepsilon^{2}a^{1-c\sigma}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{31}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{2}a^{1-c\sigma}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}_{\text{not present for }L=2}

Next, we replace the high order curvature term as follows, using the commuted rescaled Hamiltonian constraint equation (
2.36d
) that
\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma
is tracefree and symmetric:

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-2a(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\text{{Ric}}[G],\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-2(N+1)a^{-3}\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}-2(N+1)\frac{\dot{a}}{a}|\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma|_{G}^{2}+\langle\mathfrak{H}_{L,Border}+\mathfrak{H}_{L,Junk}^{\parallel},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

We pull the first two terms to left, only keeping the error terms on the right. After inserting the borderline and junk term estimates for the Hamiltonian constraint equations ((
11.17a
) and (
11.19c
)) and the evolution equation itself ((
11.17h
) and (
11.19k
)), as well as bounding
\lvert\partial_{t}G^{-1}\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-3}
and inserting (
3.17h
) as usual, we obtain (
6.14
) by integrating.
∎

Additionally, we can exploit the structure of the momentum constraint equations to gain an elliptic estimate for
\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
. Crucially, the upper bound only depends on
\Sigma
-, scalar field and Bel-Robinson energies up to order
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and appropriately small and time-scaled curvature contributions up to order
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1
. This will allow us to close the argument since we do not need to consider the Bel-Robinson energy at order
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1
to control
\Sigma
at that order, which would require higher order scalar field and curvature energies.

Lemma 6.10

(Odd order energy estimate for the second fundamental form)
.

For any
L\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
2\leq L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{2}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\phi,\cdot)+a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\varepsilon a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\,.

For
L=0
, one analogously has

(6.16)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\lesssim\,\left(a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

We prove the statement for
L=2
, since the proof of (
6.16
) is entirely analogous.

By
[
AM04
, (A.22)]
, since
(\Sigma_{t},g)
is a three-dimensional compact Riemannian manifold for any
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, any tracefree
(0,2)
tensor
U_{ij}
on
(\Sigma_{t},g)
satisfies

(6.17)

\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert\nabla U\rvert_{g}^{2}+3\text{{Ric}}[g]\cdot U\cdot U-\frac{R[g]}{2}\lvert U\rvert_{g}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert\text{{curl}}U\rvert_{g}^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\lvert\text{{div}}_{g}U\rvert_{g}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{g}\,.

In particular, for
U=\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma
and after rescaling, this reads:

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+3{\left(\text{{Ric}}[G]^{\sharp}\right)^{i}}_{j}{\left(\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma^{\sharp}\right)^{j}}_{l}{\left(\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma^{\sharp}\right)^{l}}_{i}-\frac{R[G]}{2}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\frac{3}{2}\cdot\lvert\text{{div}}_{G}\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}+a^{2}\lvert\text{{curl}}\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

The last two terms on the left hand side can be estimated by
(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}})\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,\cdot)
in absolute value using the strong
C_{G}
-norm estimate (
4.4f
). Thus, inserting the Laplace-commuted rescaled momentum constraint equations (
2.36a
) and (
2.36b
), we obtain for a suitable constant
K>0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)-K\left(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,\cdot)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left\{\lvert\Psi+C\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\phi\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{G}^{2}\left\lvert\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\mathfrak{M}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Junk}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}\left.+a^{-4}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\lvert\nabla\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\left\lvert\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{2}-2}\text{{Ric}}[G]\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\left\lvert\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Junk}\right\rvert_{G}^{2}\,\right\}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

After rearranging, using the strong
C_{G}
-norm estimates (
4.2a
), (
4.4e
), (
4.2b
) and (
4.4b
) and multiplying by
a^{4}
on both sides, we get

\displaystyle a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\phi,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(W,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{2}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{2}a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+a^{4}\|\mathfrak{M}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Junk}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+a^{4}\|\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Junk}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}\,.

The statement follows inserting the estimates (
11.19a
) and (
11.19b
).
∎

6.4.
Energy estimates for the curvature

To control commutator errors, we will also need some additional estimates on curvature energies. Unlike the other energies, these inequalities do not rely on any delicate structure within the equations and instead just rely on pointwise estimates, the Young inequality and near-coercivity of energies in the sense of Lemma
4.5
. For the sake of convenience, we phrase these estimates for
\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
since this is the order needed when improving behaviour of the total energy at order
L
.

Lemma 6.11

(Curvature energy estimates at even orders)
.

Let
L\in 2\mathbb{Z}
,
4\leq L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}16\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Then, one has

(6.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{8-c\sigma}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\Bigr\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,s)\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\Bigr\}\,ds\,.

Additionally, the following holds:

(6.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\text{{Ric}},t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\text{{Ric}},t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,s)\right)ds

Proof.

First, we note that

\|\text{{div}}_{G}^{\sharp}\nabla\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\lesssim\|\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}

holds using the low order version of (
4.14a
) with
\mathfrak{T}=\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\Sigma
for
l=2
, and similarly

\|\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\lesssim\|\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(N,\cdot)}

using (
4.13b
) at order
2
. Now, using
\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}G=0
for
L\geq 4
, we continue as usual by applying (
2.40a
) to the expression below:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\Bigr\{a^{-3}\left(\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}+\lvert\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\Sigma\rvert_{G}\right)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left(\lvert\nabla^{2}\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}N\rvert_{G}+\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}N\rvert_{G}\right)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\,+\left(\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Border}\rvert_{G}+\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Junk}\rvert_{G}\right)\cdot\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\,+a^{-3}\Sigma\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\ast\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]+N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}-1}\text{{Ric}}[G]\rvert_{G}^{2}\Bigr\}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

Due to the estimates above as well as (
4.2b
) and (
3.17h
), this implies

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-3}\left[\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)}\right]\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}

\displaystyle\,+a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(N,\cdot)}\right]\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}

\displaystyle\,+\left(\|\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Border}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Junk}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\,.

Using Corollary
5.5
at order
L
and distributing terms containing
\mathcal{E}^{(L-3)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
with (
3.8
) as usual, we get

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}+a^{8-c\sigma}\right]\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle\,+\left[\varepsilon^{\frac{31}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{2}a^{8-c\sigma}\right]\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)\right]

\displaystyle\,+\left(\|\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Border}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|\mathfrak{R}_{L-2,Junk}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}\,.

(
6.18
) now follows inserting the borderline and junk term estimates (
11.17i
) and (
11.19l
) and applying the lapse energy estimates from Corollary
5.5
.

(
6.19
) follows almost identically by inserting (
2.33
) instead of (
2.40a
) as well as (
2.28a
) for the additional
\partial_{t}G\ast(\text{{Ric}}[G]+\nicefrac{{2}}{{9}}G)
-terms. These can be estimated as

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}a^{-3}\Sigma\ast(\text{{Ric}}[G]+\frac{2}{9}G)+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}N\cdot G\ast(\text{{Ric}}[G]+\frac{2}{9}G)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-3}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(N,\cdot)}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)}\,,

which can be treated as at higher orders.
∎

Lemma 6.12

(Odd order curvature energy estimate)
.

For
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in 2\mathbb{N}
,
4\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following holds:

(6.20)

\displaystyle a(t)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1)}(\text{{Ric}},t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t_{0})^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1)}(\text{{Ric}},t_{0})

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\cdot a(s)^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(\Sigma,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\Bigr\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\phi,s)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,s)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\Sigma,s)\right)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\Bigr\}ds

Proof.

The proof is very similar to that of Lemma
6.11
since we didn’t exploit any structure within (
2.40a
) that doesn’t equally occur in (
2.40b
), and thus we omit the details. As in the proof of Lemma
6.4
, we note that the differences within the estimate come from how top order lapse terms are treated: The scaling of the top order energy allows one to estimate
a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+1)}(N,\cdot)
by scalar field energies and
\Sigma
-energies of up to order
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and curvature energies up to order
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-3
.
∎

6.5.
Sobolev norm estimates for metric objects

To close the bootstrap argument, we need to improve the behaviour of metric quantities in addition to the energy formalism, both to capture the intrinsic behaviour of the metric and to relate energies to supremum norms.

Lemma 6.13

(Sobolev norm estimates for Christoffel symbols)
.

Let
U
be a coordinate neighbourhood on
M
, viewed as a coordinate neighbourhood on
\Sigma_{t}
for
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. For any
l\in\mathbb{N},\,l\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}15\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, the following Sobolev estimate then holds:

(6.21)

\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{G}(U)}\lesssim a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{l+1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l+1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)

Proof.

Commuting the evolution equation (
2.34
) with
\nabla^{J}
, we get for
J\in\mathbb{N}
,
J\leq 17
:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{U}\Bigr[(\partial_{t}G^{-1})\ast G^{-1}\ast\dots\ast G^{-1}\ast G\ast\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\ast\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(G^{-1})\ast\dots\ast(G^{-1})\ast\partial_{t}G\ast\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\ast\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+\left(a^{-3}\sum_{I_{N}+I_{\Sigma}=J+1}\nabla^{I_{N}}(N+1)\ast\nabla^{I_{\Sigma}}\Sigma+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\nabla^{J+1}N\right)\ast\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+2\langle[\partial_{t},\nabla^{J}](\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}),\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\rangle_{G}-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left\lvert\nabla^{J}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\right\rvert_{G}^{2}\Bigr]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

We recall that (
4.4c
) implies

(6.22)

\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C^{11}_{G}}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

by (
3.5
). It follows from inserting this in (
11.10b
) along with (
4.2b
), (
4.4b
) and (
3.17h
) that

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t},\nabla^{J}](\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J}_{G}}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|N\|_{H^{J}_{G}}+\varepsilon a^{-3}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{J-1}_{G}}

is satisfied. Consequently and using the same strong
C_{G}
-norm bounds along with Lemma
4.1
, the differential inequality becomes

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\left(\|N\|_{H^{J+1}_{G}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J+1}_{G}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|N\|_{H^{J}_{G}}^{2}

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{J-1}_{G}}^{2}\,.

Further, we analogously get

-\partial_{t}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{H^{1}_{G}}^{2}+\|N\|_{H^{1}_{G}}^{2}\right)\,

and thus, with the Gronwall lemma and (
2.7
),

\displaystyle\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{H^{1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|N\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}\right)\,ds\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{H^{1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|N\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}\right)\right)\,.

This proves (
6.21
) for
l=0
, and we assume for an iterative argument that the statement has been proved for
l=J-1
. Then, we obtain (estimating the error terms in
\Sigma
and
N
by their supremum immediately):

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\left(\|N\|_{H^{J}_{G}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J}_{G}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}+4}a^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\sup_{s\in(\cdot,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{J-1}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J-1}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\,.

After integrating, applying the Gronwall lemma and dealing with the first line as before, we get

\displaystyle\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{J}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\sup_{s\in(\cdot,t_{0}]}\left(\|N\|_{H^{J}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{46}{8}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{6}{8}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\sup_{s\in(\cdot,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{J-1}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{J-1}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\,,

where the second line can obviously be absorbed into the first up to constant. Combining this with the assumption yields (
6.21
) for
l=J
and thus iteratively for all
l\leq 17
.
∎

Lemma 6.14

(Sobolev norm estimates for the metric)
.

For any
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
and any
l\in\mathbb{N},\,l\leq 18
, we have:

(6.23)

\|G-\gamma\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}^{2}\lesssim a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)

Proof.

For
l=0
, we compute the following using (
2.28a
) and (
2.28b
):

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\Bigr\{-2(\partial_{t}G^{-1})^{i_{1}j_{1}}(G^{-1})^{i_{2}j_{2}}(G-\gamma)_{i_{1}i_{2}}(G-\gamma)_{j_{1}j_{2}}-2\langle\partial_{t}G,G-\gamma\rangle_{G}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-3N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert G-\gamma\rvert_{G}^{2}\,\Bigr\}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\Bigr\{(N+1)a^{-3}\left[\Sigma\ast(G-\gamma)+\Sigma\right]\ast(G-\gamma)+N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert G-\gamma\rvert_{G}^{2}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-4N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\langle G,G-\gamma\rangle_{G}\Bigr\}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

We apply (
4.2b
) and (
3.17h
) and get

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3}\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+a^{-3}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}+\|N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}\right)\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\|N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}\right)\,.

After integrating and applying the Gronwall lemma (as well as the initial data assumption), we obtain

\displaystyle\|G-\gamma\|_{L^{2}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|N\|_{L^{2}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds\right)\,.

The statement for
l=0
now follows taking the supremum over the norms under the integral and applying (
2.7
). This extends to higher orders via the same iteration argument as in Lemma
6.13
.

∎

7.
Big Bang stability: Improving the bootstrap assumptions

In this section, we combine the energy estimates obtained in the last two sections to improve the boostrap assumptions for the energies themselves, and then show how this improves the behaviour of the solution norms. For an outline of the energy improvement arguments that we perform in Section
7.1
, we refer back to Remark
6.1
.

Before carrying out the improvements themselves, we quickly collect an estimate that shows that combining Lemma
6.6
and Lemma
6.8
yields sufficient control on the energies themselves:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
L\in 2\mathbb{N}
. Then, the following estimate is satisfied:

(7.1)

16\pi C^{2}a^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}+8\pi C^{2}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}+6\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\geq 0

Proof.

First, we recall that
N+1>0
holds by Lemma
4.1
. Additionally, we can apply (
2.8
) and the Young inequality and get the following:

\displaystyle\left\lvert 16\pi C^{2}a^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,16\pi C^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{3}{4\pi C^{2}}}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,4(N+1)\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot\left(\sqrt{3}\cdot\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}\right)\cdot\left(\sqrt{4\pi C^{2}}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,6\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}+8\pi C^{2}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}\,.

∎

This shows that
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
is controlled by the sum of the left hand sides of the inequalities in Lemma
6.6
and
6.8
for
L\in 2\mathbb{N},0\leq L\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

7.1.
Improving energy bounds

Proposition 7.2

(Improved energy bounds)
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions (see Assumption
3.16
) and the initial data assumptions (
3.10
), the following improved estimates hold on
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
:

(7.2a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\phi,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.2b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(\Sigma,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.2c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}(W,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.2d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 16)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.2e)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 16)}(N,\cdot)+a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(17)}(N,\cdot)+a^{8}\mathcal{E}^{(18)}(N,\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{8-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

We prove this estimate by performing an induction over even energy orders. Starting at order
0
, we first observe that by Lemma
7.1
, we can bound the (base level) total energy

\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{total}:=\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)

by the sum of the left hand side of (
6.2
), the left hand side of (
6.10
) weighted by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
and the left hand side of (
6.13
) weighted by
4\pi C^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
, and the left hand sides of (
6.9
) and
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot
(
6.15
) extended to
L=0
.
11
11

11

We need to weight
\mathcal{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
in the total energy by
K\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
for some
K>0
to balance out the
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}
-weight from the scalar field energy on the right hand side of (
6.13
). The weight on the Bel-Robinson energy is then needed to obtain the cancellation in Lemma
7.1
. The additional weight on
a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
is needed so that the div-curl-estimates only generates a term of size
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}_{total}
that can be absorbed later in the argument.
Combining said estimates and inserting the initial data assumption from (
3.11
), the following holds in total:

(7.3)

\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{total}(s)\,ds

Applying the Gronwall lemma (see Lemma
11.1
) to (
7.3
), we get for some suitable constant
c^{\prime}>0
:

\mathcal{E}^{(0)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}\exp\left(c^{\prime}\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,ds\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Now assume that, for
L\in 2\mathbb{N},2\leq L\leq 18
, we have already shown

(7.4a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(W,\cdot)\right)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

on
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Note that (
7.1
) means this holds true for
L=2
after updating
c>0
. Further, if
L\geq 4
holds, we assume:

(7.4b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

We will show that these assumptions hold at
L=4
after having shown the induction step for
L=2
.
We define, for
2\leq L\leq 18
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(L)}_{total}:=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(21)}(\Sigma,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\,.

We combine the respective energy estimates with the appropriate scalings
12
12

12

The weights on all terms beside the curvature energies are necessary for the same reasons as at order
0
. We need to scale the curvature energy at order
L
by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
to account for
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a^{-3}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
in (
6.18
), and the weight on the
L+1
-order curvature energy again needs to be chosen according to that on
\mathcal{E}^{(L+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)
.
, namely (in the listed order) (
6.3
), (
6.11
), (
6.14
), (
6.9
), (
6.15
), (
6.18
) and (
6.20
). Observe that the sum of these scaled left hand sides control
\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}
by Lemma
7.1
. Combining all of these estimates and inserting the initial data assumption (
3.11
), we get the following estimate:

(7.5a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(s)\,ds

(7.5b)

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)+\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)\right]\right.

(7.5c)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\bigr\{}+\varepsilon^{\frac{17}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(W,s)+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{\frac{11}{8}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)}_{\text{if }L=4}\bigr\}\,ds

(7.5d)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(a(t)^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,t)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi,t)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(W,t)

(7.5e)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}a^{4}\mathcal{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},t)+\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,t)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,t)

(7.5f)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},t)

We briefly summarize which inequalities contain the listed error term bounds as explicit terms: The first two terms in (
7.5b
) come from (
6.18
) and the latter from (
6.11
), those in (
7.5c
) from (
6.3
) and (
6.18
), and finally the last three lines are precisely the scaled right hand side of (
6.15
). Regarding the curvature energies in the various individual energy estimates, any summand with
\mathcal{E}^{(L-3)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
can be split using (
3.8
), the resulting summands containing
\mathcal{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
can always be absorbed in to the total energy term in the first line, and anything with
\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
is tracked in
\langle\text{Err}\rangle_{L}
for
L\geq 4
.

Inserting (
7.4a
)-(
7.4b
), (
7.5b
)-(
7.5c
) can be bounded up to constant by
\varepsilon^{\frac{33}{8}}a^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
. Here, the error term dominating all others arises from

\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)\,.

Regarding (
7.5d
)-(
7.5f
), notice that the first four summands can be bounded from above by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(t)
up to constant. For the remaining three terms, we can again insert the induction assumptions (
7.4a
)-(
7.4b
), bounding them by
\varepsilon^{\frac{17}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
.

In summary and after rearranging, (
7.5a
)-(
7.5f
) becomes for some constant
K>0
:

\displaystyle(1-K\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}})\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(s)\,ds+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{33}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{17}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{total}^{(L)}(s)\,ds\,

The prefactor on the left hand side is positive for small enough
\varepsilon>0
, and the Gronwall lemma then yields

(7.6)

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

In particular, this directly implies that the induction assumptions (
7.4a
) and (
7.4b
), using (
3.9
) to cover the skipped odd order, hold at order
L
, completing the induction step, and clearly also that (
7.4b
) holds for
L-2=2
using to (
7.6
) at order
2
. This completes the induction argument, proving (
7.2a
)-(
7.2d
).
Finally, applying the obtained improved estimates for
\nabla\phi
and
\text{{Ric}}[G]
to Corollary
5.8
, we also get (
7.2e
).
∎

7.2.
Improving solution norm control

To close the bootstrap argument, it now remains to show that the improved energy bounds also imply improved bounds for
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
. The former follows almost directly using Lemma
4.5
:

Corollary 7.3

(Improved Sobolev norm bounds)
.

On
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following estimates hold:

(7.7a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.7b)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{H^{18}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.7c)

\displaystyle\|N\|_{H^{18}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

First, we apply the improved energy estimates from Proposition
7.2
as well as the strong
C_{G}
-norm bounds from Lemma
4.3
to the near-coercivity estimates in Lemma
4.5
. With this, we directly obtain the following Sobolev norm estimates (updating
c
):

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{H_{G}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{H^{18}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\|\text{{Ric}}[G]+\frac{2}{9}G\|_{H^{16}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\|\bm{E}\|_{H^{18}_{G}}^{2}+\|\bm{B}\|_{H^{18}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

By Lemma
6.5
, we also have

(7.8)

\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{18}_{G}}\lesssim\left(1+\varepsilon a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|\Sigma\|_{H^{19}_{G}}+\varepsilon\|\Psi\|_{H^{19}_{G}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

We take particular care in showing that the improved bound holds for
a^{2}\|\nabla\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{19}_{G}}
: First, note that (
7.2d
) implies
\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 17)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
. Applying this along with (
4.4e
) to (
4.13d
), as well as (
4.13a
) in the second line and (
7.2a
) as well as (
7.8
) in the final step, we obtain:

\displaystyle a^{4}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{19}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{4}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sum_{m=0}^{9}\|\nabla^{2}\Delta^{m}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 17)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\Delta^{9}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\|\Delta^{10}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{17}_{G}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot\mathcal{E}^{(\leq 19)}(\phi,\cdot)+a^{4-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{17}_{G}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Further, inserting (
7.2a
), (
7.2b
) and (
7.2d
) into Corollary
5.5
implies

\displaystyle\,a^{8}\|\Delta^{10}N\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{G}}+a^{4}\|\nabla\Delta^{9}N\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{G}}+\sum_{m=0}^{9}\|\Delta^{m}N\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{11}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

and subsequently, applying Lemma
4.5
as before,

a^{8}\|N\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{20}_{G}}+a^{4}\|N\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{19}_{G}}+\|N\|^{2}_{H^{18}_{G}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

Finally, having now shown (
7.7b
) and (
7.7c
), we can apply these to (
6.23
) to get

\|G-\gamma\|^{2}_{H^{18}_{G}}\lesssim a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{15}{4}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}+4}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,,

proving (
7.7a
).
∎

Intuitively, the bounds on
\mathcal{C}
should now follow from
\mathcal{H}
by the standard Sobolev embedding. However, since both of these norms are with respect to
G
, the embedding constant may be time dependent. To circumvent this issue, we need to switch between norms with repsect to
G
and
\gamma
and then apply the embedding with respect to
C_{\gamma}
and
H_{\gamma}
. The following lemma ensures that we still obtain bootstrap improvements after performing these norm switches:

Lemma 7.4

(Moving between norms)
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N},\,l\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}18\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\zeta
be a scalar field,
\mathfrak{T}
be an arbitrary
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent tensor. Then, for some multivariate polynomial
P_{l}
with
P_{l}(0,0)=0
, we have

(7.9a)

\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}_{G}(U)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\zeta\|_{C_{\gamma}^{\max\left\{0,\lfloor\frac{l-1}{2}\rfloor\right\}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\cdot P_{l}\left(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{l-1}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})},\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{C^{l-1}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right)

(7.9b)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C_{\gamma}^{\max\left\{0,\lfloor\frac{l-1}{2}\rfloor\right\}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\cdot P_{l}\left(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})},\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right)

as well as the same inequalities with the roles of
G
and
\gamma
reversed. For
l\leq 12
, this reduces to:

(7.10a)

\displaystyle a^{c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\zeta\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}

(7.10b)

\displaystyle a^{c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\,

Further, one has

(7.11a)

\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\zeta\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}+a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\|\zeta\|_{C_{G}^{\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}(\Sigma_{t})}^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{l-1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l-1}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)\,,

(7.11b)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}+a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C_{G}^{\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}(\Sigma_{t})}^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)

Remark 7.5
.

While we only need the tensorial inequalities for gradient vector fields and
(0,2)
-tensors when applied to norms in
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
, the proof is more simple when considering tensors of arbitrary rank.

Proof.

We restrict ourselves to proving the tensorial statements; the scalar field analogues follow analagously except for the fact that, since
\nabla_{i}\zeta=\hat{\nabla}_{i}\zeta=\partial_{i}\zeta
, error terms caused by Christoffel symbols always enter at one order less. Thus, it remains to show (
7.9b
), (
7.10b
) and (
7.11b
) by iterating over derivative order.

Starting with the base level estimates, we have if
\mathfrak{T}
is of rank
(r,s)
:

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}-\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}=

\displaystyle\,\Bigr[G_{i_{1}j_{1}}\dots G_{i_{r}j_{r}}(G^{-1})^{p_{1}q_{1}}\dots(G^{-1})^{p_{s}q_{s}}-\gamma_{i_{1}j_{1}}\dots\gamma_{i_{r}j_{r}}(\gamma^{-1})^{p_{1}q_{1}}\dots(\gamma^{-1})^{p_{s}q_{s}}\Bigr]\cdot

\displaystyle\,\cdot{\mathfrak{T}^{i_{1}\dots i_{r}}}_{p_{1}\dots p_{s}}{\mathfrak{T}^{j_{1}\dots j_{r}}}_{q_{1}\dots q_{s}}

We successively replace
G^{\pm 1}
by
(G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1})+\gamma^{\pm 1}
, take the
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\gamma}
-norm of each factor and use (
4.4c
)-(
4.4d
). This yields

\left\lvert\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}-\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\,,

implying
(
7.9b
) (and (
7.10b
)) for
l=0
after rearranging and taking supremums suitably.

To show (
7.11b
) at base level, consider

\displaystyle\,\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}-\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\left(\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}-\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}+\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\lvert\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\frac{\mu_{G}-\mu_{\gamma}}{\mu_{\gamma}}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\gamma}\,.

We can control the first summand on the right hand side as before, while we have
\lvert\mu_{G}-\mu_{\gamma}\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon
by (
4.11
). Hence,

(1-K\varepsilon)\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}})\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}

follows for a suitable constant
K>0
, implying the statement for small enough
\varepsilon>0
.

Next, we perform the iteration for (
7.9b
), assuming the statement and the analogue with
\gamma
and
G
reversed to hold up to order
