l-1
. As above, note that

\left\lvert\,\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{G}^{2}-\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lvert\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}-\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert\,

where we can rewrite the second term as

\left\lvert 2\langle\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T},\hat{\nabla}\mathfrak{T}\rangle_{\gamma}-\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert

and hence obtain (moving between pointwise norms as before)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\,\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{G}^{2}-\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\,.

Regarding
\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}
, we have the following schematic decomposition :

(7.12)

\displaystyle\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{I=0}^{J-1}\hat{\nabla}^{J-I-1}(\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma})\ast_{\gamma}\left(\nabla^{I}\mathfrak{T}+\hat{\nabla}^{I}\mathfrak{T}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\langle\text{at least cubic nonlinear terms}\rangle\,,

Here,
\ast_{\gamma}
encodes the analogous schematic product notation with regards to
\gamma
(see subsection
2.1.8
). Regarding the Christoffel symbols, notice (
7.9b
) with roles of
\gamma
and
G
reversed holding up to
l-1
implies that, for any
m\in\{0,\dots,l-1\}
and some multivariate polynomial
\tilde{P}_{m}
, we have

\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C_{\gamma}^{m}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\tilde{P}_{m}(\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C_{G}^{m}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})},\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})},\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{C^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})})\,.

As explained in Remark
3.7
, we can bound
\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C_{G}^{m}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}
by a polynomial in
\|G-\gamma\|_{C_{G}^{m+1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}
. Hence, we can apply (
4.4c
) to obtain

(7.13)

\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C_{\gamma}^{l-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

Moving back to (
7.12
) and just considering the first line for now, this implies the following:

(7.14)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{\dot{C}^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}-\|\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{\dot{C}_{\gamma}^{l}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}+\sum_{m=0}^{l-1}\|\nabla^{m}\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\left(\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}+\sum_{m=0}^{\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}\|\nabla^{m}\mathfrak{T}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}\right)\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C^{l-1}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}^{2}\,

\displaystyle\,+\langle\text{at least cubic nonlinear terms}\rangle

We can rewrite
\nabla^{m}\mathfrak{T}
-norms in
C_{\gamma}
as ones in
C_{G}
up to
a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
as before. Then, we can apply the already obtained estimates up to order
l-1
show that the first two lines of the right hands side can be estimated by the right hand side of (
7.9b
). The highly nonlinear terms can be dealt with similarly, closing the induction over admissible
l
. (
7.10b
) immediately follows by applying (
4.4c
)-(
4.4d
) and (
7.13
).

Now, assume (
7.11b
) to be proven up to order
J-1
. By analogous arguments as at order zero, we get after rearranging:

\int_{M}\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\gamma}\lesssim\left\lvert\int_{M}\left(\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}-\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert+\int_{M}\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\,,

so we only need to concern ourselves with the first summand. Reversing roles of
G
and
\gamma
compared to the proof of (
7.9b
), we get

\left\lvert\,\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{G}^{2}-\left\lvert\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}\right\rvert_{G}^{2}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lvert 2\langle\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T},\nabla\mathfrak{T}\rangle_{G}-\lvert\nabla^{J}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{J}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right\rvert,

and have the following, applying Lemma
6.13
immediately to estimate
\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{l-1}_{G}}
:

\displaystyle\,\left\rvert\int_{V}\left\{2\langle\nabla^{l}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{l}\mathfrak{T},\nabla\mathfrak{T}\rangle_{G}-\lvert\nabla^{l}\mathfrak{T}-\hat{\nabla}^{l}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right\}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}+\|\hat{\nabla}^{\leq l-1}\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{H^{0}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\left(\|\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{C^{\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}+\|\hat{\nabla}^{\leq\lceil\frac{l-1}{2}\rceil}\mathfrak{T}\|^{2}_{C^{0}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\,\quad\cdot a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)

By the same arguments as earlier, we have
\|\hat{\nabla}^{\leq l-1}\mathfrak{T}\|_{H^{l-1}_{G}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{H^{l-1}_{\gamma}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}
and can then apply the induction hypothesis. This proves (
7.11b
).
∎

Corollary 7.6

(Improved
C
-norm bounds)
.

On
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following estimate is satisfied:

(7.15)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

We first apply the Sobolev norm estimates in Lemma
7.4
to (
7.7a
), to then control
\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}
via the standard Sobolev embedding
H^{l+2}_{\gamma}(M)\hookrightarrow C^{l}(M)
, and finally control
\mathcal{C}
with (
7.9a
)-(
7.9b
).

Note that by Lemma
4.3
, we can control the
C_{G}
-norm up to order
10
of every quantity occurring in
\mathcal{H}
beside the lapse by at worst
\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
, while the bootstrap assumption already implies better behaviour for the lapse. Thus, we can apply (
7.11a
)-(
7.11b
) to every norm appearing in
\mathcal{H}
, and obtain by applying (
7.7b
) and (
7.7c
) in the second line:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}+\varepsilon a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{s\in(t,t_{0})}\left(\|N\|_{H^{18}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{18}_{G}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\varepsilon a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\varepsilon^{4}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

In particular, we can update
c
such that

\lvert P(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})},\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})})\rvert\lesssim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

holds for any multivariate polynomial
P
that appears when applying (
7.9a
)-(
7.9b
). Again using the strong
C_{G}
-norm estimates from Lemma
4.3
, this then implies
\mathcal{C}\lesssim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
.
∎

8.
Big Bang stability: The main theorem

In this section, we provide the proof of the first main result, Theorem
1.1
, which we state in more detail in Theorem
8.2
below. As in
[
RS18b
,
Spe18
]
, most of the work has already been done by establishing the necessary bounds on solution norms.

Remark 8.1

(Existence of a CMC hypersurface)
.

As mentioned in Section
1.2.1
, it may seem that the generality of the results in Theorem
8.2
is restricted by taking the initial data on
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
to be CMC. However, as long as one remains close enough to a constant time hypersurface of the FLRW reference metric (which is CMC), one can locally evolve the perturbed data in harmonic gauge to a nearby hypersurface that is CMC and remains close to the FLRW reference solution. To make this a bit more precise, and also since this it is a little less involved than the arguments in
[
RS18b
]
, we will briefly sketch how the arguments from
[
FK20
, Section 2.5]
extend to our setting.

First, we once again assume without loss of generality that our initial data is sufficiently regular. Note that we can locally evolve our data within harmonic gauge to get a
C^{17}
-regular family of metrics with near-FLRW initial data (for well-posedness, consider the analogue of
[
RS18b
, Proposition 14.1]
). Consider the Banach manifold
\mathcal{M}^{17}
formed by the set of
C^{17}
Lorentz metrics on
I\times\overline{M}
for an open interval
I
around
t_{0}
such that the surfaces of constant time are Riemannian, endowed with the norm

\|\tilde{g}\|=\|\tilde{n}^{2}\|_{C^{17}_{dt^{2}+\gamma}(I\times M)}+\|\tilde{X}\|_{C^{17}_{dt^{2}+\gamma}(I\times M)}+\|\tilde{g}_{t}\|_{C^{17}_{dt^{2}+\gamma}(I\times M)}\,,

where
\tilde{g}\in\mathcal{M}^{17}
has lapse
\tilde{n}
, shift
\tilde{X}
and spatial metrics
(\tilde{g}_{t})_{t\in I}
. Further, for any
f\in C^{17}(M,I)
, we define the embedding
\iota_{f}:M\hookrightarrow\overline{M}
by
x\mapsto(f(x),x)
, and subsequently define the smooth map

\displaystyle H_{0}:\mathcal{D}:=\{(\tilde{g},f)\in\mathcal{M}^{17}\times C^{17}(M,I)|\iota_{f}^{\ast}\tilde{g}\text{ is Riemannian}\}\longrightarrow C^{16}(M)

\displaystyle(\tilde{g},f)\mapsto\text{mean curvature of }(M,\tilde{g}_{t})\text{ embedded along }\iota_{f}\,.

One easily checks that
(\overline{g}_{FLRW},t_{0})
is a regular point of
H_{0}
. By the implicit function theorem for Banach manifolds, this means there is a (unique) smooth function
F
that maps an open neighbourhood of
\overline{g}_{FLRW}
in
\mathcal{M}^{17}
to an open neighbourbood of the constant function
x\mapsto t_{0}
in
C^{16}(M)
such that
H_{0}(\cdot,F(\cdot))=\tau(t_{0})
holds in that neighbourhood.

Thus, we can choose a surface
\Sigma^{\prime}
with mean curvature
\tau(t_{0})
near the original
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
. Furthermore, for small enough
\varepsilon>0
, the initial data on
\Sigma^{\prime}
remains close to the FLRW initial data in the sense of Assumption
3.10
, using similar arguments to control Sobolev norms. Thus, we can replace
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
by
\Sigma^{\prime}
without loss of generality, proving that the CMC assumption (
2.10
) is not a true restriction.

Theorem 8.2

(Stability of Big Bang formation)
.

Let
(M,\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi})
be initial data to the Einstein scalar-field system as discussed in Section
1.2.1
. Further, let the data be embedded into a time-oriented 4-manifold such that it induces initial data for the rescaled solution variables (see Definition
2.9
) at the initial hypersurface
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
. We also assume this rescaled initial data is close to that of the FLRW reference solution (see (
2.1
) and (
2.2
)) in the sense that

(8.1)

\mathcal{H}(t_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\mathcal{H}_{top}(t_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\mathcal{C}(t_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{2}

is satisfied (with
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
as in Definition
3.6
).
13
13

13

Essentially, this translates to smallness in
H_{\gamma}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
C_{\gamma}^{17}
. For
\varepsilon=0
, the solution is the FLRW reference solution.

Then, the past maximal globally hyperbolic development
((0,t_{0}]\times M,\overline{g},\phi)
of this data within the Einstein scalar-field system (
1.1a
)-(
1.1c
) in CMC gauge (
2.10
) with zero shift is foliated by the CMC hypersurfaces
\Sigma_{s}=t^{-1}(\{s\})
, and one has

(8.2)

\mathcal{H}(t)+\mathcal{C}(t)+\mathcal{C}_{\gamma}(t)\lesssim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

for some
c>0
and any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
. In particular, this implies the following statements:

Asymptotic behaviour of solution variables:
We denote the solution metric as
\overline{g}=-n^{2}dt^{2}+g
, the second fundamental form (viewed as a
(1,1)
-tensor) with respect to
\Sigma_{t}
as
k
and the volume form with regards to
g
on
\Sigma_{t}
by
{\text{{vol}}}_{g}
. There exist a smooth function
\Psi_{Bang}\in C_{\gamma}^{15}(M)
, a
(1,1)
-tensor field
K_{Bang}\in C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
and a volume form
{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\in C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
such that the following estimates hold for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
:

(8.3a)

\displaystyle\|n-1\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

(8.3b)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{-3}{\text{{vol}}}_{g}-{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

(8.3c)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{3}\partial_{t}\phi-(\Psi_{Bang}+C)\right\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

(8.3d)

\displaystyle\left\|\phi-\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot(\Psi_{Bang}+C)\right\|_{\dot{C}^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&1\leq l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

(8.3e)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{3}k-K_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

Further, these footprint states satisfy the equations
14
14

14

These are precisely the (generalized) Kasner relations, see Section
1.2.3
.

(8.4a)

\displaystyle{(K_{Bang})^{a}}_{a}=

\displaystyle\,-\sqrt{12\pi}C\,,

(8.4b)

\displaystyle 8\pi(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{2}+{(K_{Bang})^{a}}_{b}{(K_{Bang})^{b}}_{a}=

\displaystyle\,12\pi C^{2}

and remain close to the data of the reference solution in the following sense, where
\mathbb{I}
denotes the Kronecker symbol:

(8.5a)

\displaystyle\left\|{\text{{vol}}}_{\gamma}-{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(8.5b)

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(8.5c)

\displaystyle\left\|K_{Bang}+\sqrt{\frac{4\pi}{3}}C\mathbb{I}\right\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\,

Additionally, there exists a
(0,2)
-tensor field
M_{Bang}\in C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
satisfying

(8.6)

\left\|M_{Bang}-\gamma\right\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon

and, with
\odot
and
\exp
meant in the matrix product and exponential sense respectively, one has

(8.7)

\left\|g\odot\exp\left[\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\right)\cdot K_{Bang}\right]-M_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\,.\end{cases}

Moreover, the Bel-Robinson variables
E
and
B
satisfy the following estimates:

(8.8a)

\displaystyle\|E\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.8b)

\displaystyle\|B\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\begin{cases}\varepsilon a^{-2-c{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 15\\
\varepsilon a^{-4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 16\end{cases}

Causal disconnectedness:
Let
\bm{\alpha}
be a past directed causal curve on
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(0,t]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times M,\overline{g})
for
t\leq t_{0}
with domain
[s_{1},s_{max})
such that
\bm{\alpha}(s_{1})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{t}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
s_{max}
is maximal. Then, there exists a constant
\mathcal{K}>0
that does not depend on
\bm{\alpha}
such that one has

(8.9)

L[\bm{\alpha}]=\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{max}}\sqrt{(\gamma_{ab})_{\bm{\alpha}(s)}\dot{\bm{\alpha}}^{a}(s)\dot{\bm{\alpha}}^{b}(s)}\,ds\leq\mathcal{K}a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,,

where
\gamma
is the negative Einstein spatial reference metric on
M
(see Definition
2.1
).

Geodesic incompleteness:
Let
\bm{\alpha}
be a past directed, affinely parametrized causal geodesic emanating from
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
with parameter time
\mathcal{A}
normalized to
\mathcal{A}(t_{0})=0
. Then,

(8.10)

\mathcal{A}(0)\leq\mathcal{K}_{1}\cdot\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(t_{0})\rvert\cdot a(t_{0})^{1+K_{2}\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-\mathcal{K}_{2}\varepsilon}\,ds<\infty\,,

holds for suitable constants
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}>0
that are independent of
\bm{\alpha}
, and thus any such geodesic crashes into the Big Bang hypersurface in finite affine parameter time. Hence, for sufficiently points
p,q\in\Sigma_{t}
with
\text{dist}_{\gamma}(p,q)>2\mathcal{K}a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
, the causal pasts of
p
and
q
cannot intersect.

Blow-up:

The norm
\lvert k\rvert_{g}
behaves toward the Big Bang hypersurface as follows:

(8.11a)

\left\|a^{6}\lvert k\rvert_{g}^{2}-(K_{Bang})^{i}_{j}(K_{Bang})^{j}_{i}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{4-c{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Further, with
W[\overline{g}]
denoting the Weyl curvature and
P[\overline{g}]=\text{{Riem}}[\overline{g}]-W[\overline{g}]
,

(8.11b)

\left\|a^{12}P_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}P^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}-\frac{5}{3}\cdot(8\pi)^{2}(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{4}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{4-c{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,

is satisfied, whereas there exists a scalar footprint
W_{Bang}\in C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
such that one has

(8.11c)

\left\|a^{12}W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}W^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}-W_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{0}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{2-c{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

Here,
W_{Bang}
is a fourth order polynomial in
\hat{K}_{Bang}=K_{Bang}+\sqrt{\frac{4\pi}{3}}C\mathbb{I}
and
\Psi_{Bang}
and satisfies

(8.11d)

\|W_{Bang}\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon\,.

Finally, the scalar curvature
R[\overline{g}]
and the Ricci curvature invariant
\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]_{\alpha\beta}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]^{\alpha\beta}
blow-up with the asymptotics

(8.11e)

\displaystyle\|a^{6}R[\overline{g}]-8\pi(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{2}\|_{C^{0}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,\text{and}

(8.11f)

\displaystyle\|a^{12}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]_{\alpha\beta}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]^{\alpha\beta}-(8\pi)^{2}(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{4}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,,

and the Kretschmann scalar
\mathcal{K}=\text{{Riem}}[\overline{g}]_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\text{{Riem}}[\overline{g}]^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}
exhibits stable blow-up in the following sense:

(8.11g)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\|a^{12}\mathcal{K}-\frac{5}{3}\cdot(8\pi)^{2}(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{4}-W_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{0}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Remark 8.3

(The solution variables exhibit AVTD behaviour)
.

The estimates (
8.3a
)-(
8.3e
) and (
8.7
) imply that the solution is asymptotically velocity term dominated (AVTD) in the sense that, toward the Big Bang singularity, they behave at leading order like solutions to the (formal) velocity term dominated equations. These arise by dropping any terms containing spatial derivatives in the decomposed Einstein system, i.e. in (
2.15a
), (
2.15b
), (
2.17a
) and (
2.18
).

Proof.

As argued at the end of Section
3.4
, we can assume without loss of generality that our initial data is sufficiently regular. Hence, the local existence statement in Lemma
3.14
and the initial data requirements (
8.1
) ensure that there exists a local solution to the Einstein scalar-field system on
[t_{1},t_{0}]\times M
and that the bootstrap assumption (see Assumption
3.16
) holds on
[t_{1},t_{0}]\times M
with
t_{1}\in(0,t_{0})
and
\sigma=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{16}}
Let
\mathfrak{t}\in(0,t_{0})
be such that
(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]\times M
is the maximal domain on which the solution variables exist and satisfy the bootstrap assumptions. For contradiction, we now assume that
\mathfrak{t}>0
were to hold.

Due to Corollary
7.6
, there exist (summarizing all updates) constants
c_{1},K_{1}>0
such that, for any
t\in(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]
,

(8.12)

\mathcal{C}(t)\leq K_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a(t)^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,

If
\varepsilon
is small enough such that
K_{1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}<K_{0}
and
c_{1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}<c_{0}\sigma
hold, this is a strict improvement of the bootstrap assumption. Furthermore, argued exactly as in the proof of
[
RS18b
, Theorem 15.1]
, above improvement ensures none of the blow-up criteria of Lemma
3.14
are satisfied if
\mathfrak{t}>0
were to hold, essentially as a direct consequence of (
8.12
). Hence, the solution could be classically extended to a CMC hypersurface
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{t}}
diffeomorphic to
M
while satisfying the improved estimates by continuity, and further to an interval
(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime},t_{0}]
for some
0<\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}<\mathfrak{t}
on which the bootstrap assumptions must then be satisfied, also by continuity. This contradicts the maximality of
(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]
.

Thus, the rescaled solution variables induce a unique solution to the Einstein scalar-field system on
(0,t_{0}]\times M
such that (
8.12
) is satisfied for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
. The core estimate (
8.2
) follows since Corollary
7.3
and Corollary
7.6
now hold on
(0,t_{0}]
.

From (
8.2
), the asymptotic behaviour in (
8.3a
)-(
8.3e
) and (
8.7
) is established as in
[
RS18b
, Theorem 15.1]
, which we briefly outline: First, we note that (
8.3a
) follows directly from (
8.2
). For the remaining estimates, the arguments are similar, so consider for example
\partial_{t}\phi
: By the rescaled wave equation (
2.32a
) and (
8.3a
), we have that

\left\|\partial_{t}\Psi\right\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\varepsilon a^{1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

Hence, for an arbitrary decreasing sequence
(t_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}},
on
(0,t_{0}]
that converges to zero, we have

\|\Psi(t_{m_{1}},\cdot)-\Psi(t_{m_{2}},\cdot)\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t_{m_{1}})^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t_{m_{1}})^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

for any
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N},\,m_{1}<m_{2}
by (
2.6
). This shows that
\Psi(t_{m_{1}},\cdot)
is a Cauchy sequence in
C_{\gamma}^{15}(M)
and hence there exists a limit function
{\Psi}_{Bang}\in C_{\gamma}^{15}(M)
that satisfies

\|\Psi(t,\cdot)-({\Psi}_{Bang}-C)\|_{C^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\begin{cases}\varepsilon a(t)^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l\leq 14\\
\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}&l=15\end{cases}

for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
. Since
\Psi=a^{3}n^{-1}\partial_{t}\phi-C
holds by definition, (
8.3c
) now follows by examining the Taylor expansion of
n^{-1}-1
at
0
using (
8.3a
).

(
8.4a
) follows directly from the CMC condition (
2.10
), the asymptotic behaviour (
8.3e
) of
a^{3}k
and the Friedman equation (
2.3
). (
8.4b
) follows from asymptotic limit of the Hamiltonian constraint (
2.16a
) with (
2.3
), (
8.3a
), (
8.3c
) and (
8.3e
) as well as (
8.2
) for lower order terms. (
8.7
) follows exactly as in
[
RS18b
, Theorem 15.1]
, and (
8.5a
)-(
8.6
) are a direct result of the initial data assumptions and applying the respective asymptotic estimates to
t=t_{0}
.

For the first estimate in (
8.8b
), we apply the momentum constraint (
2.29d
) to get

\lvert\nabla^{J}B\rvert_{G}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-4}\lvert\nabla^{J}\bm{B}\rvert_{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a^{-2}\lvert\nabla^{J}\text{{curl}}_{G}\Sigma\rvert_{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lesssim a^{-2}\lvert\nabla^{J+1}\Sigma\rvert_{G}

and consequently, with Lemma
7.4
as well as (
4.4g
) and (
8.2
),

\displaystyle\|B\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|B\|_{C^{15}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}+\varepsilon a^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot P_{{15}}(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\Sigma\|_{C^{16}_{G}(\Sigma_{t})}+\varepsilon a^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot P_{15}(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(\Sigma_{t})})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

The remaining estimates in (
8.8a
) and (
8.8b
) are contained in (
8.2
). The results (
8.9
) and (
8.10
) follow as in the proofs of (15.6) and (15.7) in
[
RS18b
, Theorem 15.1]
from the asymptotic behaviour of the solution variables in (
8.3a
)-(
8.3e
) and (
8.7
). We briefly sketch the proof of (
8.10
): Consider a geodesic
\bm{\alpha}
affinely parametrized by
\mathcal{A}
as in the statement. The geodesic equations then lead to the following estimate for some suitable
\mathcal{K}>0
:

\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\rvert\leq\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rvert+\mathcal{K}\left[\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\lvert N\rvert+n^{-1}\lvert\partial_{t}N\rvert+n^{-1}\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{g}+n\lvert\hat{k}\rvert_{g}\right]\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rvert\,.

The leading term is hereby arises from the mean curvature condition. Arguing as with the elliptic estimates in Section
5
, one can show that
\lvert\partial_{t}N\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
. Thus, along with the other pointwise bounds on
n
,
g
and
\hat{k}
, one obtains

\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\rvert\leq\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\left(1+c\varepsilon\right)\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rvert

and consequently
\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(t)\rvert\leq\lvert\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(t_{0})\rvert a^{-1-c\varepsilon}
by the Gronwall lemma. (
8.10
) follows by integrating.

Turning to the blow-up behaviour of geometric invariants, observe (
8.11a
) is a direct consequence of (
8.3e
). Regarding (
8.11c
), we first compute
using (
2.19
) and standard algebraic manipulations that

a^{12}W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}W^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=a^{12}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lvert E\rvert_{g}^{2}+8\lvert B\rvert_{g}^{2}\right)=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lvert\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}+8\lvert\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,.

By the rescaled constraint equation (
2.29c
), we have

\bm{E}_{ij}=-\dot{a}a^{2}\Sigma_{ij}+(\Sigma\odot\Sigma)_{ij}-\left[\frac{8\pi}{3}\Psi^{2}+\frac{16\pi}{3}C\Psi\right]G_{ij}+\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon a^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\right)\,,

for
t\downarrow 0
. Further, by expanding (
2.3
) around
a=0
, we have

\dot{a}a^{2}=\sqrt{\frac{4\pi}{3}}C+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{2}\right)
.
Since
\Sigma^{\sharp}
and
\Psi
converge to footprint states
\hat{K}_{Bang}=K_{Bang}+\sqrt{\frac{4\pi C}{3}}\mathbb{I}
and
\Psi_{Bang}
in
C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
respectively, this shows that
W_{Bang}\in C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)
exists, is a fourth order polynomial in
\hat{K}_{Bang}
and
\Psi_{Bang}
, and satisfies

\left\|\lvert\bm{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{0}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

as well as where (
8.11d
). Due to (
8.8b
), the
\lvert\bm{B}\rvert_{G}^{2}
-term in the Weyl curvature scalar is negligible in comparison, and thus (
8.11c
) immediately follows.

Furthermore, one has

\displaystyle P_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}P^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=

\displaystyle\,2\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]_{\alpha\beta}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]^{\alpha\beta}-\frac{2}{9}R[\overline{g}]^{2},\,

and the statement follows once more with (
8.3c
), (
8.3a
) as well as (
8.2
) for lower orders.
∎

9.
Future stability

The goal of this section is to show the following theorem:

Theorem 9.1

(Future stability of Milne spacetime)
.

Let the rescaled initial data
(\bm{g},\bm{k},\nabla\phi,\phi^{\prime})
on
M
be sufficiently close to
(\gamma,\frac{1}{3}\gamma,0,0)
in
H^{5}\times H^{4}\times H^{4}\times H^{4}
on some initial hypersurface
\Sigma_{\tau=\tau_{0}}
(see Definition
9.4
and Assumption
9.7
). Then, its maximal globally hyperbolic development
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\phi)
within the Einstein scalar-field system in CMCSH gauge is foliated by the CMC Cauchy hypersurfaces
(\Sigma_{\tau})_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},0)}
, is future (causally) complete and exhibits the following asymptotic behaviour:

(\bm{g},\bm{k},\phi^{\prime},\nabla\phi)(\tau)\longrightarrow(\gamma,\frac{1}{3}\gamma,0,0)\text{ as }\tau\uparrow 0

Since the control of geometric perturbations uses the same arguments as in
[
AF20
]
, the focus in this section will lie on dealing with the the scalar field. The key idea is controlling decay of the scalar field using an indefinite corrective term on top of the canonical energy (see Definition
9.6
).

9.1.
Preliminaries

9.1.1.
Notation, gauge and spatial reference geometry

Within this section, we will decompose the Lorentzian metric as follows:

(9.1a)

\overline{g}=-n^{2}dt^{2}+g_{ab}(dx^{a}+X^{a})(dx^{b}+X^{b}dt)

We impose CMCSH gauge (see
[
AM04
]
) via

(9.1b)

t=\tau,\,g^{ij}(\Gamma^{a}_{ij}-\hat{\Gamma}^{a}_{ij})=0\,,

where
\hat{\Gamma}
refers to the Christoffel symbols with regards to the spatial reference metric
\gamma
.

We extend the notation from the Big Bang stability analysis regarding foliations, derivatives, indices and schematic term notation to this setting (see Section
2.1
). In particular,
\Sigma_{T}
and
\Sigma_{\tau}
will refer to spatial hypersurfaces along which the logarithmic time
T
(see (
9.2c
)) and the mean curvature
\tau
are constant (see (
9.2c
) on why these are interchangeable), and we will write for example
\Sigma_{T=0}
when inserting a specific value to avoid potential ambiguity. We use similar notation for scalar functions and tensors that depend on
T
or, respectively,
\tau
.

For the extent of the future stability analysis, we have to introduce an additional condition for the spatial geometry beyond Definition
2.1
:

Definition 9.2

(Spectral condition for the Laplacian of the spatial reference manifold)
.

Let
\mu_{0}(\gamma)
to be the smallest positive eigenvalue of the Laplace operator
-\Delta_{\gamma}=(\gamma^{-1})^{ab}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}
acting on scalar functions, where
(M,\gamma)
is as in Definition
2.1
.
(M,\gamma)
additionally is assumed to satisfy

\mu_{0}(\gamma)>\frac{1}{9}\,.

Remark 9.3

(Manifolds that satisfy Definition
9.2
)
.

The existing literature on spectra of
\Delta_{\gamma}
usually considers hyperbolic manifolds with sectional curvature
\kappa=-1
, and thus one needs to verify that
\mu_{0}
is strictly greater than
1
to verify the analogue of Definition
9.2
after rescaling.

Numerical work, e.g.,
[
CS99
,
Ino01
]
, provides evidence for over 250 compact hyperbolic
3
-manifolds to satisfy this spectral bound, many of which are closed. In particular, both
[
CS99
]
15
15

15

These results have to be interpreted cautiously since the numerical method cannot detect eigenvalues below
1
.
and
[
Ino01
]
consider the smallest closed orientable hyperbolic
3
-manifold, the Weeks space m003(-3,1), and compute that it falls under Definition
9.2
with
\mu_{0}\approx 27,8
in
[
CS99
, Table IV]
and
26\lessapprox\mu_{0}\lessapprox 27,8
in
[
Ino01
, p. 639, Table 2]
. Moreover, as demonstrated in
[
Ino01
, p. 642, Figure 6]
, many manifolds with small enough diameter
d
satisfy this condition. In fact, this is ensured by, for example, the analytical bound

\mu_{0}\geq\max\left\{\frac{\pi^{2}}{2d^{2}}-\frac{1}{2},\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{4}}{d^{4}}+\frac{1}{4}}-\frac{3}{2},\frac{\pi^{2}}{d^{2}}e^{-d}\right\}\,\,

(cf.
[
CZ95
, Theorem 1.1-1.2]
with
L=2
) which implies
\mu_{0}>10
for Weeks space, which has diameter
d\approx 0.843
. Furthermore,
[
Ino01
]
finds no closed hyperbolic manifold violating this bound. We also note that it is conjectured that, for any arithmetic hyperbolic 3-manifold, one at least has
\mu_{0}\geq 1
(see
[
Ber03
, Conjecture 2.3]
). In fact, this is tied to the Ramanujan conjecture for automorphic forms. Finally, one can construct compact hyperbolic manifolds where
\mu_{0}
becomes arbitrarily small, see
[
Cal94
, Corollary 4.4]
.

9.1.2.
Rescaled variables and Einstein equations

We will use the standard rescaling of the solution variables by
\tau
:

Definition 9.4

(Rescaled variables for future stability)
.

(9.2a)

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{ij}=\tau^{2}g_{ij}\,,(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ij}=\tau^{-2}g^{ij}\,,\ \bm{\Sigma}_{ij}=\tau\hat{k}_{ij}

(9.2b)

\displaystyle\bm{n}=\tau^{2}n\,,\ \hat{\bm{n}}=\frac{\bm{n}}{3}-1\,,\ \bm{X}^{a}=\tau X^{a}

Furthermore, we introduce the logarithmic time

(9.2c)

T=-\log\left(\frac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}\right)\,\Leftrightarrow\,\tau=\tau_{0}e^{-T}

which satisfies
\partial_{T}=-\tau\partial_{\tau}
. Toward the future,
\tau
increases from
\tau_{0}
toward
0
, and thus
T
increases from
0
to
\infty
. We additionally introduce:

(9.2d)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\partial_{0}}=\partial_{T}+\mathcal{L}_{\bm{X}}=-\tau(\partial_{\tau}-\mathcal{L}_{X})

(9.2e)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}=\bm{n}^{-1}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi=n^{-1}(-\tau)^{-1}(\partial_{\tau}-\mathcal{L}_{X})\phi

Moreover, for any scalar function
\zeta
, we denote by
\overline{\zeta}
the mean integral with regards to
(\Sigma_{T},\bm{g}_{T})
.

For symmetric
(0,2)
-tensors
h
, we define the perturbed Lichnerowicz Laplacian

(9.3)

\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}h_{ab}=-\frac{1}{\mu_{\bm{g}}}\hat{\nabla}_{k}\left((\bm{g}^{-1})^{kl}\mu_{\bm{g}}\hat{\nabla}_{l}h_{ab}\right)-2\text{{Riem}}[\gamma]_{akbl}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{kk^{\prime}}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ll^{\prime}}h_{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\,.

This operator satisfies

(9.4)

\left(\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]-\text{{Ric}}[\gamma]\right)_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}(\bm{g}-\gamma)_{ij}+J_{ij},\quad\|J_{ij}\|_{H^{l-1}}\lesssim\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l}}\,,

see
[
AM03
, Pf. of Theorem 3.1]
. Under our conditions for the reference geometry,
[
Krö15
]
implies that the smallest positive eigenvalue of
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}
, denoted by
\lambda_{0}
, satisfies
\lambda_{0}\geq\frac{1}{9}
, and that
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}
has trivial kernel. The spectral condition in Definition
9.2
is not necessary for this to hold true.

We now collect the 3+1-decomposition of the Einstein scalar-field equations in CMCSH gauge with the help of
[
AF20
, (2.13)-(2.18)]
:

Lemma 9.5

(Rescaled CMCSH equations)
.

The rescaled CMCSH Einstein scalar-field equations take the following form:

The constraint equations

(9.5a)

R[\bm{g}]-\lvert\bm{\Sigma}\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}-\frac{2}{3}=8\pi\left[\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right],\ \text{{div}}_{\bm{g}}\bm{\Sigma}_{a}=8\pi\tau^{3}\phi^{\prime}\nabla_{b}\phi,

the elliptic lapse and shift equations

(9.5b)

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{\bm{g}}-\frac{1}{3}\right)\bm{n}=

\displaystyle\,\bm{n}\left(\lvert\bm{\Sigma}\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}+4\pi\left[\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right]\right)-1,

(9.5c)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\bm{g}}\bm{X}^{a}+(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ab}\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]_{bm}\bm{X}^{m}=

\displaystyle\,2(\bm{g}^{-1})^{am}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{bn}\nabla_{b}\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\Sigma}_{mn}-(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ab}\nabla_{b}\hat{\bm{n}}+8\pi\bm{n}\tau^{3}\phi^{\prime}\nabla_{b}\phi

\displaystyle\,-2(\bm{g}^{-1})^{bk}((\bm{g}^{-1})^{cl}\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\Sigma}_{bc}-\nabla_{b}\bm{X}^{l})(\Gamma^{a}_{kl}-\hat{\Gamma}^{a}_{kl})\,,

the geometric evolution equations

(9.5d)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\bm{g}_{ab}=

\displaystyle\,2\bm{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}+2\hat{\bm{n}}\bm{g}_{ab}\,\,,

(9.5e)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\partial_{0}}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ab}=

\displaystyle\,-2\bm{n}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ac}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{bd}\bm{\Sigma}_{cd}-2\hat{\bm{n}}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ab}\,,

(9.5f)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}=

\displaystyle\,-2\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}-\bm{n}\left(\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]_{ab}+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}_{ab}\right)+\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\bm{n}

\displaystyle\,+2\bm{n}\cdot(\bm{g}^{-1})^{mn}\bm{\Sigma}_{am}\bm{\Sigma}_{bn}-\frac{1}{3}\hat{\bm{n}}\bm{g}_{ab}-\hat{\bm{n}}\Sigma_{ab}-8\pi\bm{n}\nabla_{a}\phi\nabla_{b}\phi

and the wave equation

(9.5g)

\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}=\langle\nabla\bm{n},\nabla\phi\rangle_{\bm{g}}+\bm{n}\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi+(1-\bm{n})\phi^{\prime}\,.

9.1.3.
Energies and data assumptions

The proof will rely on the following corrected energy quantities:

Definition 9.6

(Energies for future stability)
.

(9.6a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{l}\int_{M}\left[\phi^{\prime}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{l}\phi^{\prime}-\phi\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{l+1}\phi\right]{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}},\qquad\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(l)}=(-1)^{l}\int_{M}(\phi-\overline{\phi})\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{l}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

(9.6b)

\displaystyle E_{SF}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m=0}^{4}\left(\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(m)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(m)}\right)

(9.6c)

\displaystyle E_{\text{geom}}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m=1}^{5}\biggr(\frac{9}{2}\int_{M}\langle\bm{g}-\gamma,\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}^{m}(\bm{g}-\gamma)\rangle_{\bm{g}}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\langle 6\bm{\Sigma},\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}^{m-1}(6\bm{\Sigma})\rangle_{\bm{g}}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\sum_{m=1}^{l}}+c_{E}\int_{M}\langle 6\bm{\Sigma},\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}^{m-1}(\bm{g}-\gamma)\rangle_{\bm{g}}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\biggr)

The constant
c_{E}
is given by

(9.7)

c_{E}=\begin{cases}1&\lambda_{0}>\frac{1}{9}\\
9(\lambda_{0}-\delta^{\prime})&\lambda_{0}=\frac{1}{9}\,,\end{cases}

where
\delta^{\prime}>0
is chosen to be small enough within the argument.

The Sobolev norms
H_{\bm{g}}^{l}
and
C_{\bm{g}}^{l}
are defined analogously to Definition
3.3
and
3.4
, with similar conventions on surpressing time dependence in notation whereever possible. Since norms with respect to
\bm{g}
and
\gamma
are equivalent under the bootstrap assumption (and consequently throughout the entire argument), we will simply denote the norms by
H^{l}
and
C^{l}
throughout unless the specific metric is crucial.

Assumption 9.7

(Initial data assumption)
.

The initial data on the spatial hypersurface
\Sigma_{T=0}
is assumed to be small in the following sense:

(9.8)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{C^{3}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{2}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{5}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{6}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{H^{6}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{4}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\,\leq\delta^{2}

Remark 9.8

(Local well-posedness toward the future)
.

Under the above initial data assumption, local well-posedness is satisfied by analogizing the arguments for local well-posedness in the vacuum setting (see
[
AM03
, Theorem 3.1]
) with the matter coupling added. Since this only consists of adding another wave equation to the hyperbolic system, the argument is structurally unchanged given appropriate smallness assumptions on
\phi^{\prime}
and
\nabla\phi
(where
\phi
itself does not enter into the Einstein system). As before, we can without loss of generality assume that the initial is sufficiently regular to ensure that
E_{geom},\,\mathcal{E}^{(l)}_{SF}
and
\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(l)}
initially are continuously differentiable (in time) for any
l\leq 4
.

Assumption 9.9

(Bootstrap assumption)
.

We assume that, on the bootstrap interval
T\in[0,T_{Boot})
, one has

(9.9)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{C^{3}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{2}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{5}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{6}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{H^{6}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{4}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\leq\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We only choose not to use
“
\lesssim
”
-notation in the above assumptions for notational convenience in some technical computations. As before,
\delta
can be chosen to have been sufficiently small for the following estimates to hold and for the decay estimates we derive from the bootstrap assumptions to be strict improvements. Moreover, note that (
9.9
) is satisfied since all of the norms are continuous in time (see Remark
9.8
)

Before moving on to the energy estimates, we quickly collect the following immediate consequence of the bootstrap assumptions:

Lemma 9.10

(Sobolev estimate for the curvature)
.

The following estimate holds for any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

(9.10a)

\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{l}}\lesssim\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l+2}}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l+1}}^{2}

Under the bootstrap assumptions, this implies

(9.10b)

\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{C^{1}}+\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{3}}\lesssim\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}

Proof.

By (
9.4
), one has

\displaystyle\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{l}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}(\bm{g}-\gamma)\|_{H^{l}}+K\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l+1}}^{2}\,

for some suitably large
K>0
and that
\mathcal{L}_{\bm{g},\gamma}
is elliptic. This implies the first inequality, while the latter follows from directly from the bootstrap assumption (
9.9
) and by applying the standard Sobolev embedding.
∎

9.2.
Elliptic estimates

We briefly collect the elliptic estimates for lapse and shift:

Lemma 9.11

(Elliptic estimates for lapse and shift)
.

Let
l\in\{3,4,5,6\}
. Then, one has
\bm{n}\in(0,3)
(thus
\hat{\bm{n}}\in(-1,0)
) and the following estimates hold:

(9.11a)

\displaystyle\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{l}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\delta^{2}e^{-T}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l-2}}+\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l-2}}\right]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(9.11b)

\displaystyle\|\bm{X}\|_{H^{l}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l-1}}+\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l-2}}\right]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

The pointwise bounds on
\bm{n}
follow via (
9.5b
) and the maximum principle as in Lemma
4.1
. For the remaining estimates, applying elliptic regularity theory to (
9.5b
) and (
9.5c
) implies:

\displaystyle\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{l}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{\lfloor\frac{l-2}{2}\rfloor}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}^{2}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l-2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\left[\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}\left(1+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{C^{2}}\right)+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2}}\right]\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l-2}}\right]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\|\bm{X}\|_{H^{l}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{\lfloor\frac{l-2}{2}\rfloor}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l-1}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{1}}\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l-3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\left[\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}^{2}\left(1+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{C^{2}})+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2}}\right)\right]\left[1+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2}}\right]\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{l-2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l-2}}\right]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

The statement then follows by inserting (
9.9
).
∎

9.3.
Scalar field energy estimates

9.3.1.
Near-coercivity of
E_{SF}

We will be able to prove a decay estimate via a Gronwall argument only for the corrected energy
E_{SF}
. Hence, we first need to verify that this energy controls the solution norms, for which we first show that it controls the
“canonical”
scalar field energies:

Lemma 9.12

(Positivity of corrected scalar field energies)
.

Let

Q=\frac{\sqrt{1+9q}-1}{\sqrt{1+9q}}\ \text{with}\ q=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{0}(\gamma)-\frac{1}{9}\right)\,.

Then, for any
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
and
\delta>0
small enough, one has

(9.12)

Q\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}\leq\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(l)},\quad\text{hence}\quad Q\sum_{m=0}^{4}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}\leq E_{SF}

Proof.

We denote the smallest positive eigenvalue of
\Delta_{\bm{g}}
acting on scalar functions on
\Sigma_{T}
as
\mu_{0}(\bm{g}_{T})
. By the bootstrap assumption (
9.9
) and since
\mu_{0}
depends continuously on the metric, we obtain the following for small enough
\delta>0
:

\mu_{0}(\bm{g}_{T})\geq\mu_{0}(\gamma)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{0}(\gamma)-\frac{1}{9}\right)\geq\frac{1}{9}+q

By the Poincaré inequality applied on
(\Sigma_{T},\bm{g}_{T})
(see
[
CBM01
, p.1037]
), above spectral bound implies the following for any
\zeta\in H^{1}(\Sigma_{T})
:

(9.13)

\|\zeta-\overline{\zeta}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}(\Sigma_{T})}^{2}\leq\mu_{0}(\bm{g}_{T})^{-1}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}(\Sigma_{T})}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{9}+q\right)^{-1}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}(\Sigma_{T})}^{2}

For
l=0
, this means

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}\geq

\displaystyle\,\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}^{2}-\frac{2}{3}\|\phi-\overline{\phi}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}^{2}-2\left(1+9q\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\sqrt{1+9q}-1}{\sqrt{1+9q}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}\,.

For
l=1
, notice that we can rewrite
\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(1)}
as follows:

\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(1)}=\int_{M}\langle\nabla\phi,\nabla\phi^{\prime}\rangle_{\bm{g}}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla\phi,\nabla\left(\phi^{\prime}-\overline{\phi^{\prime}}\right)\right\rangle_{\bm{g}}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=-\int_{M}\left(\phi^{\prime}-\overline{\phi^{\prime}}\right)\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

Hence, applying (
9.13
) to
\zeta=\phi^{\prime}
yields

\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(1)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(1)}\geq\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(1)}-2\left(1+9q\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}\|\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{1+9q}-1}{\sqrt{1+9q}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(1)}\,.

For
l=2,3,4
, notice
\overline{\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi}=\overline{\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi^{\prime}}=\overline{\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{2}\phi}=0
holds due to the divergence theorem, hence the argument proceeds as in
l=0,1
.
∎

Lemma 9.13

(Near-coercivity of corrected scalar field energy)
.

For any scalar function
\zeta
and
k\in\{1,2\}
, one has the following under the bootstrap assumptions:

\displaystyle\int_{M}\lvert\nabla^{2}\zeta\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}\zeta\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}+\lvert\nabla\zeta\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\zeta\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left(\|\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-1}}^{2}+\|\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-2}}^{2}\right)+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2k-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\zeta\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left(\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-1}}^{2}+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-2}}^{2}\right)+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{C^{2}}^{2}\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2k-2}}^{2}

Consequently, the following estimate holds:

(9.14)

\displaystyle\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{4}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,E_{SF}^{(4)}+\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}

Proof.

The inequalities for
\zeta
follows from the same arguments as Lemma
4.5
, except that we have
\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]\|_{C^{1}_{\bm{g}}}\lesssim 1+\delta\lesssim 1
by Lemma
9.10
. The final estimate then follows by applying these estimates to
\zeta=\phi^{\prime}
and
\zeta=\phi
and applying Lemma
9.12
.
∎

9.3.2.
Preparations for energy estimates

Before proving the energy estimate, we need to establish two technical lemmas: First, we collect a formula to differentiate integrals, and then some estimates needed to deal with the mean value of
\phi
in the base level correction term.

Lemma 9.14

(Differentiation of integrals, future stability version)
.

For any diffentiable function
\zeta
, one has

(9.15)

\partial_{T}\int_{M}\zeta{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=\int_{M}\left(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\zeta+3\hat{\bm{n}}\zeta\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\,.

Proof.

As in the proof of (
4.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\int_{M}\zeta{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\partial_{T}\zeta+\frac{\partial_{T}\mu_{\bm{g}}}{\mu_{\bm{g}}}\zeta\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\partial_{T}\zeta+3\hat{\bm{n}}\zeta-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{g}^{-1})^{ab}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{X}}\bm{g}_{ab}\zeta\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\partial_{T}\zeta+3\hat{\bm{n}}\zeta-\text{{div}}_{\bm{g}}\bm{X}\cdot\zeta\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

The statement now follows by applying Stokes’ theorem to the final term and rearranging.
∎

Lemma 9.15

(Decay estimate for the integrated time derivative)
.

For any
T>0
, we have

(9.16)

\int_{\Sigma_{T}}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=\left(\int_{\Sigma_{T=0}}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right)\cdot e^{-2T}\,.

Consequently, the bootstrap assumptions imply

(9.17)

\left\lvert\int_{\Sigma_{T}}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right\rvert\lesssim\,\delta^{3}e^{-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{5}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}

for
\delta>0
small enough.

Proof.

Using that the integral of
\text{{div}}_{\bm{g}}(\bm{n}\nabla\phi)
vanishes, we compute:

\partial_{T}\left(\int_{M}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right)=\int_{M}\left(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\phi^{\prime}\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=\int_{M}\left[(1-\bm{n})\phi^{\prime}+(\bm{n}-3)\phi^{\prime}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}=-2\left(\int_{M}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right)

Hence, (
9.16
) precisely describes the solution to this ODE (
f^{\prime}=-2f
) with prescribed initial value at
T=0
, and the initial data assumption (
9.8
) implies

\left\lvert\int_{M}\phi^{\prime}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right\rvert\leq\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}(\Sigma_{T=0})}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}(\Sigma_{T=0})e^{-2T}\leq\delta^{2}e^{-2T}\,.

Furthermore, one has by (
9.15
) that

(9.18)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial_{T}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\left(\Sigma_{T}\right)=\int_{\Sigma_{T}}3\hat{\bm{n}}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Consequently, one has

\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}=\left[-\frac{\partial_{T}{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\left(\Sigma_{T}\right)}{{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\left(\Sigma_{T}\right)}\cdot\overline{\phi}+\frac{1}{{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\left(\Sigma_{T}\right)}\int_{M}\left(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi+3\hat{\bm{n}}\phi\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\right]=\int_{M}\left(\bm{n}\phi^{\prime}+3\hat{\bm{n}}(\phi-\overline{\phi})\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}\,.

By applying
\lvert\bm{n}\rvert<3
, the adapted Poincare inequality (
9.13
) and the bootstrap assumptions (
9.9
), this implies

\lvert\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}\rvert\lesssim\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{L^{2}_{\bm{g}}}\lesssim\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\,.

(
9.17
) now follows by combining this with (
9.16
).
∎

9.3.3.
Energy estimates

Now, we can collect the following estimates for the corrected scalar field energies:

Lemma 9.16

(Base level estimate for the corrected scalar field energy)
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions, the following estimate holds for some
K>0
:

(9.19)

\partial_{T}E_{SF}^{(0)}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2+E_{SF}^{(0)}+K\delta e^{-T}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{SF}^{(0)}}\sqrt{\left[\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]}+K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

We compute, using
[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\nabla]\phi=0
,
\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi=\bm{n}\phi^{\prime}
and the rescaled wave equation (
9.5g
):

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[2\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}\cdot\phi^{\prime}+2\left\langle\nabla\phi,\nabla\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi\right\rangle_{\bm{g}}+\left(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\bm{g}^{-1}\right)^{ab}\nabla_{a}\phi\nabla_{b}\phi+3\hat{\bm{n}}\left(\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right)\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr[2\left(\langle\nabla\bm{n},\nabla\phi\rangle_{\bm{g}}+\bm{n}\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi+(1-\bm{n})\phi^{\prime}\right)\phi^{\prime}-2(\bm{n}\phi^{\prime})\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi

\displaystyle\,\,\phantom{\int_{M}}-2\bm{n}\langle\bm{\Sigma},\nabla\phi\nabla\phi\rangle_{\bm{g}}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\hat{\bm{n}}\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\biggr]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

With
2(1-\bm{n})=-4-6\hat{\bm{n}}
, integration by parts and using the bootstrap assumption (
9.9
) on
C
-norms, we get for some constant
K>0
that we update from line to line:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-4\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+K\left[\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{0}}\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{1}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}}+\left(\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{M}-4\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{1}}}

Similarly and using the same evolution equations, we obtain:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi\cdot\phi^{\prime}-\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}\cdot\phi^{\prime}+(\phi-\overline{\phi})\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}+3\hat{\bm{n}}(\phi-\overline{\phi})\phi^{\prime}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[3\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\left(\phi-\overline{\phi}\right)\cdot\text{{div}}_{\bm{g}}\left(\bm{n}\nabla\phi\right)-2\left(\phi-\overline{\phi}\right)\phi^{\prime}-\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,-2\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}+\int_{M}3\left[\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}-3\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+3\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{0}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}-\int_{M}\left(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\overline{\phi}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\right)\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

Applying Lemma
9.15
to the last term, we get:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Combining these two estimates, inserting (
9.11a
) and (
9.12
) as well as updating
K
yields:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}E_{SF}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{T}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}+\frac{2}{3}\partial_{T}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[\left(-4+\frac{2}{3}\cdot 3\right)\lvert\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}-\frac{2}{3}\cdot 3\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}-2\cdot\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{0}

\displaystyle\,+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(0)}}\sqrt{\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{1}}}+K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,-2E_{SF}^{(0)}+K\delta e^{-T}\sqrt{E^{(0)}_{SF}}\left(\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{E^{(0)}_{SF}}\right)+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

∎

Lemma 9.17

(Higher order estimates for the corrected scalar field energy)
.

For any
l\in\{1,\dots,4\}
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\left(\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(l)}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\,-2\left(\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(l)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(l)}\right)+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{l}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(m)}}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\,\qquad\cdot\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{l}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{l}}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{l}}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l}}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Proof.

Starting with
l=2k,\,k\in\{1,2\}
, one calculates:

(9.20a)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr[2\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+2\langle\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi,\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi\rangle_{\bm{g}}

(9.20b)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+(\widetilde{\partial_{0}}{\bm{g}}^{-1})^{ab}\cdot\nabla_{a}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\nabla_{b}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi+3\hat{\bm{n}}\left(\lvert\Delta^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}+\lvert\nabla\Delta^{k}\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\right)

(9.20c)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+2[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi^{\prime}\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+2\left\langle[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi,\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\right\rangle_{\bm{g}}\biggr]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

We insert the rescaled wave equation (
9.5g
) and
\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi=\bm{n}\phi^{\prime}
into the right hand side of (
9.20a
) and obtain for some constant
K>0
:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:fESF-1}\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr[-4\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}-6\hat{\bm{n}}\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+\bm{n}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k+1}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\biggr]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,+K\|\Delta^{k}\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{2k+1}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2k}}\right)

\displaystyle\,+\int_{M}\left[-\bm{n}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k+1}\nabla\phi-3\langle\nabla\hat{\bm{n}},\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\rangle_{\bm{g}}\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,+K\|\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k+1}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2k}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-4\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,+K\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}}\cdot\Bigr[\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{0}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}}\right)\cdot\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{2k+1}}+\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k}}\right)\cdot\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2k}}\Bigr]

For (
9.20b
), we use (
9.5e
) and the bootstrap assumption (
9.9
) to bound it by
K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}
. Regarding (
9.20c
), the commutator formulas (
12.1a
)-(
12.1b
) imply the following:

\displaystyle\|[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{n}\|_{C^{2k-1}}\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-1}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{2k-2}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k}}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\|[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\bm{n}\|_{C^{2k}}\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{1}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{{H}^{2k}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{2k-2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Summarizing, inserting the
C
-norm bounds from the bootstrap assumption (
9.9
) and updating
K
, this implies

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}-4\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}

\displaystyle\,+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}}\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}\right)

\displaystyle\,+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{2k+1}}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{2k}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right)

Moving on to the corrective term, we compute:

(9.21a)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(2k)}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\biggr[\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi^{\prime}

(9.21b)

\displaystyle\,\phantom{\int_{M}}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi^{\prime}+\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi^{\prime}\biggr]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

Inserting the evolution equations into the right hand side of (
9.21a
), we can bound that line by

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[3\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\right]{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+K\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2k}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k-1}}\|\Delta^{k}\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\int_{M}\left[-2\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+3\hat{\bm{n}}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}+3\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k+1}\phi+3\hat{\bm{n}}\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\cdot\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k+1}\phi\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,+K\left[\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{2k}}\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k-1}}\right)+\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{0}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}}\right)\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{2k+1}}\right]\|\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

Note that, after integrating by parts, the last two terms in the second line can be bounded by

\int_{M}-3\lvert\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{1}}(\|\nabla\Delta^{k}\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta^{k}\phi\|_{L^{2}})\|\nabla\Delta^{k}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\,.

For the terms in (
9.21b
), notice that the first term can be bounded by
\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k-1}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}}
,
while the commutator terms can be estimated as before, with

\displaystyle\|[\widetilde{\partial_{0}},\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}]\phi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{0}}\|\bm{n}\|_{C^{0}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\dot{H}^{2k-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\|\bm{n}\|_{C^{2k}}\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{C^{2k-2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Combining all of the above, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(2k)}\leq

\displaystyle\,-2\mathcal{C}_{SF}^{(2k)}+\int_{M}\left[3\lvert\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi^{\prime}\rvert-3\lvert\nabla\Delta_{\bm{g}}^{k}\phi\rvert_{\bm{g}}\right]\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle\,+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{2k}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2k}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{2k+1}}+\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{2k}}\right]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\,\phantom{+K}\cdot\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k)}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{SF}^{(2k-1)}}\right)

Finally, combining both differential estimates yields the statement for
l=2k
.
For
l=2k-1,\,k\in\{1,2\}
, the argument is completely analogous and hence omitted.
∎

9.4.
Geometric variables

We can take the following results from prior literature, where we additionally apply the elliptic estimates in Lemma
9.11
:

Lemma 9.18

(Coercivity of geometric energies,
[
AM11
, Lemma 7.4]
)
.

For sufficiently small
\delta>0
, the following estimate holds:

(9.22)

\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{4}}^{2}\lesssim E_{\text{geom}}

Lemma 9.19

(Geometric energy estimate,
[
AF20
, Lemma 20]
)
.

Let
\delta>0
be chosen appropriately small, and let

(9.23)

\alpha=\begin{cases}1&\lambda_{0}>\frac{1}{9}\\
1-3\sqrt{\delta^{\prime}}&\lambda_{0}=\frac{1}{9}\,,\end{cases}

where
\delta^{\prime}>0
is the same as in (
9.7
), in particular suitably small. Then, there exists some constant
K>0
such that the following estimate holds:

(9.24)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}E_{\text{geom}}\leq

\displaystyle\,-2\alpha E_{\text{geom}}+KE_{\text{geom}}^{\frac{3}{2}}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{E_{geom}}\left[\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{4}}\right]

9.5.
Closing the bootstrap

Now, we can collect our estimates to improve the bootstrap assumptions:

Proposition 9.20

(Improved bounds for future stability)
.

Let the bootstrap assumption (see Assumption
9.9
) be satisfied for
T\in[0,T_{Boot})
and assume the initial data holds at
T=0
(see Assumption
9.7
). For
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
\alpha
as in (
9.23
) with
\delta^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, the following estimates hold:

(9.25a)

\displaystyle\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{2}}+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{4}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{-\alpha T}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(9.25b)

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{C^{3}}+\|\Sigma\|_{C^{2}}+\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{5}}+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{4}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\alpha T}

(9.25c)

\displaystyle\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{C^{4}}+\|\hat{\bm{n}}\|_{H^{6}}+\|\bm{X}\|_{H^{6}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\delta^{3}e^{-2\alpha T}

Proof.

In the following, the positive constant
K
that may be updated from line to line.

Combining the estimate from Lemma
9.16
as well as those from Lemma
9.17
at each level with Lemma
9.19
and applying the (near)-coercivity estimates (
9.14
) and (
9.22
) to the right hand sides, we obtain:

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\left(E^{(4)}_{SF}+E_{geom}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\,-2E_{SF}+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\sqrt{E_{SF}}\left(\sqrt{E_{SF}+\delta^{2}e^{-T}\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}}+\sqrt{E_{geom}}\right)

\displaystyle\,+K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}

\displaystyle\,-2\alpha E_{geom}+KE^{\frac{3}{2}}_{geom}+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\sqrt{E_{geom}}\sqrt{E_{SF}+\delta^{2}e^{-T}\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}}

Applying (
9.10a
) to the curvature norms, as well as (
9.22
) to the resulting norms on
\bm{g}-\gamma
and (
9.9
) (which implies
\sqrt{E_{geom}}\lesssim\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}
), this becomes

\partial_{T}\left(E^{(4)}_{SF}+E_{geom}\right)\leq-2\alpha(E_{SF}^{(4)}+E_{geom})+K\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\left(E_{geom}+E_{SF}^{4}\right)+K\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{5}{2}T}\,.

and consequently, since
\alpha\leq 1
,

\partial_{T}\left[e^{2\alpha T}\left(E^{(4)}_{SF}+E_{geom}\right)\right]\lesssim\delta e^{-\frac{T}{2}}\cdot e^{2\alpha T}\left(E^{(4)}_{SF}+E_{geom}\right)+\delta^{3}e^{-\frac{T}{2}}

The Gronwall lemma, along with the initial data assumption (
9.8
), now implies

(9.26)

E^{(4)}_{SF}+E_{geom}\lesssim\delta^{3}e^{-2\alpha T}\,.

Lemma
9.18
and the standard Sobolev embedding then imply (
9.25b
). In particular, this means

(9.27)

\left\|\text{{Ric}}[\bm{g}]+\frac{2}{9}\bm{g}\right\|_{H^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lesssim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\alpha T}

due to Lemma
9.10
, and for
\delta^{\prime}>0
small enough, inserting (
9.26
) and (
9.27
) into (
9.14
) shows (
9.25a
). This in particular improves the
C
-norms we just used the bootstrap assumption for, and reinserting this improvement into (
9.14
) improves the bound to (
9.26
). Moreover, (
9.25c
) follows directly from Lemma
9.11
and the already obtained improvements.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

The problem is locally well-posed as outlined in Remark
9.8
. There then is some maximal interval
[0,T_{Boot})
for the logarithmic time
T
– or, equivalently, some maximal time interval
[\tau_{0},\tau_{Boot})
– on which the solution exists and the bootstrap assumptions (see Assumption
9.9
) are satisfied. By the analogous argument to the proof of Theorem
8.2
, the decay estimates in Proposition
9.20
are strictly stronger than the bootstrap assumptions for small enough
\delta,\delta^{\prime}>0
. This implies
T_{Boot}=\infty
(resp.
\tau_{Boot}=0
) since we could else extend the solution strictly beyond
T_{Boot}
while also satisfying the bootstrap assumptions. This proves the convergence statement in Theorem
9.1
.

Finally, the decay estimates imply that
\lvert\nabla n\rvert_{g}
, respectively
\lvert k\rvert_{g}
, are bounded by
\tau^{\alpha-1}
, respectively
\tau^{\alpha+1}
, up to constant on
[\tau_{0},\tau)
. Since
\alpha
is at worst slightly smaller than
1
, both functions are integrable on
[\tau_{0},0)
for suitably small
\delta^{\prime}>0
. By
[
CBC02
]
, this means the spacetime is future complete.
∎

10.
Global stability

To prove Theorem
1.2
, what still needs to be shown is that initial data as in Theorem
1.1
develops from
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
to some hypersurface
\Sigma_{t_{1}}\equiv\Sigma_{\tau(t_{1})}
in its future such that the data in
\Sigma_{t_{1}}
is near-Milne in the sense of Assumption
9.7
and in CMCSH gauge. From there, near-Milne stability yields the behaviour in the future of
\Sigma_{\tau(t_{1})}
, and hence future stability of near-FLRW spacetimes as in Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Within this proof,
t
will denote the
“physical”
time coordinate used throughout the Big Bang stability analysis, while
\tau
denotes the mean curvature time used within CMCSH gauge.

Consider initial data
(g,k,\nabla\phi,\partial_{0}\phi)
induced on the CMC hypersurface
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
within
\overline{M}
such that the rescaled variables are close to FLRW reference data in the sense of Theorem
8.2
. Moreover, let
(\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi})
be the geometric initial data on
M
that induce it via the embedding
\iota:M\hookrightarrow\overline{M}
.

Notice that

P:H^{20}_{\gamma}(M)\rightarrow H^{18}_{\gamma}(M),\,Y^{i}\mapsto\Delta_{\gamma}Y^{i}+(\gamma^{-1})^{il}{\text{{Ric}}[\gamma]}_{lj}Y^{j}=\Delta_{\gamma}Y^{i}-\frac{2}{9}Y^{i}=0

is an isomorphism since
\Delta_{\gamma}
has no positive eigenvalues. Hence, using
[
FK20
, Theorem 2.5, Remark 2.6]
, there is an metric
\mathring{g}^{\prime}
isometric to
\mathring{g}
that remains
H^{18}_{\gamma}(M)
-close to
\gamma
and satisfies

((\mathring{g}^{\prime})^{-1})^{ij}\left(\Gamma[\mathring{g}^{\prime}]^{k}_{ij}-\hat{\Gamma}[\gamma]^{k}_{ij}\right)=0.

Let
\theta\in\text{Diff}(M)
be the diffeomorphism such that
\theta^{\ast}\mathring{g}=\mathring{g}^{\prime}
, then the proof of
[
FK20
, Theorem 2.5]
implies that
\theta
can be chosen close to the identity map within
H^{18}(\text{Diff}(M))
, and consequently that
\theta^{\ast}\mathring{k}=\mathring{k}^{\prime},\,\theta^{\ast}\mathring{\pi}=\mathring{\pi}^{\prime}
and
\theta^{\ast}\mathring{\psi}=\mathring{\psi}^{\prime}
remain close to
\frac{1}{3}\gamma,0
and
C
in
H^{18}_{\gamma}(M)
. By the same argument as in Remark
8.1
, we can now evolve this data locally and obtain a new initial hypersurface
\Sigma^{\prime}
close to
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
that is in CMCSH gauge and that
(g,k,\nabla\phi,\partial_{0}\phi)
is close to the reference data in the sense of Assumption
3.10
, exchanging the initial time
t_{0}
by some close time
t_{0}^{\prime}
.

Since
\tau
is strictly increasing,
t\equiv t(\tau)
exists and we can interchangeably view
a
as a function in
t
or
\tau
with some abuse of notation. The Friedman equation (
2.3
) implies
\partial_{t}a\geq\frac{1}{9}
and thus
a(t)\geq\frac{1}{9}t
on
(0,\infty)
, as well as

-\tau=3\frac{\dot{a}}{a}=\frac{1}{a}+\langle\text{lower order terms}\rangle\ \text{as}\ t\to\infty\ (\text{resp. }\tau\to 0)\,.

We choose
t_{1}>\max\{1,t_{0}^{\prime}\}
large enough (resp.
\tau(t_{1})\equiv\tau_{0}
small enough) that the following estimates hold for some small
\chi\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
that depends only on
\delta
:

(10.1)

\displaystyle Ca(t_{1})^{-3}\tau(t_{1})^{-1}\leq

\displaystyle\,\chi

(10.2)

\displaystyle-\tau(t_{1})\cdot{a(t_{1})}\in

\displaystyle\,[1-\chi,1+\chi]

As the solution is Cauchy stable, i.e., it and its maximal time of existence depend continuously upon the initial data,
16
16

16

For the argument for Einstein vacuum in CMCSH gauge, see
[
AM04
, Theorem 3.1]
. As with local existence, the argument in the Einstein scalar-field system is largely identical since the only difference amounts to coupling the hyperbolic parts of the system with a further hyperbolic one.
one can choose
\varepsilon>0
in the analogue of Assumption
3.10
small enough to ensure the following: The solution exists until
t_{1}>t_{0}^{\prime}
and
(a^{-2}g,a\hat{k},\nabla\phi,a^{3}\widetilde{\partial_{0}}\phi)
remain
K\varepsilon
-close to
(\gamma,0,0,C)
in
H_{\gamma}^{6}\times H_{\gamma}^{5}\times H_{\gamma}^{5}\times H_{\gamma}^{5}
for some suitable
K>0
along the slab
\cup_{s\in[t_{0}^{\prime},t_{1}]}\Sigma_{s}
. What now remains to be shown is that this implies Assumption
9.7
in the sense that, if
\varepsilon
is small enough,
\delta
can be made as small as necessary for Theorem
9.1
to apply.

Note that the scalings in Definition
9.4
can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{g}-\gamma=(\tau\cdot a)^{2}\cdot(a^{-2}g-\gamma)+(\tau^{2}\cdot a^{2}-1)\gamma,\quad\bm{\Sigma}=\frac{\tau}{a}(a\hat{k})

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}=C\left(-\tau^{-1}\cdot a^{-3}\right)+\left(-\tau^{-1}\cdot a^{-3}\right)\cdot(a^{3}n^{-1}(\partial_{\tau}-\mathcal{L}_{X})\phi-C)

Since (
10.2
) implies
\tau\cdot a
is close to
-1
at
t_{1}
,
\|(\tau\cdot a)^{2}(a^{-2}g-\gamma)\|_{H^{6}}
can be bounded by
\frac{\delta^{3}}{2}
for small enough
\varepsilon
. Choosing
\chi<\frac{\delta^{3}}{2}
then implies
\|\bm{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{6}(\Sigma_{\tau_{0}})}<\delta^{3}
. That
\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{H^{5}}
can be made smaller than
\delta^{3}
for small enough
\varepsilon>0
follows since
\frac{\tau}{a}
behaves like
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
up to a constant by (
10.2
).

For the normal derivative of the wave, notice that
\lvert C\left(-\tau^{-1}\cdot a^{-3}\right)\rvert
is bounded by
\chi
due to (
10.1
), and that
-\tau^{-1}a^{-3}
is equivalent to
a^{-2}
by (
10.2
). Hence, we can similarly ensure that
\phi^{\prime}
is bounded in
H^{5}
by
\delta^{3}
. Since
\nabla\phi
is not changed in either rescaling, and bounds on lapse and shift (up to constant) follow from the elliptic estimates in Lemma
9.11
, it follows each individual norm in Assumption
9.7
can be bounded by
\delta^{3}
up to constants that depend only on
\gamma
, and hence the initial data assumption itself can be satisfied for suitably small
\delta>0
.

This proves that we can develop from initial data for the Big Bang stability proof to near-Milne initial data within a CMCSH foliation, and thus we obtain Theorem
1.2
from Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
9.1
.
∎

11.
Appendix – Big Bang Stability

11.1.
Basic formulas and estimates

11.1.1.
Tools from elementary calculus

Lemma 11.1

(A Gronwall lemma)
.

Let
f,\chi,\xi:[a,b]\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
be continuous functions such that
\chi\geq 0
,
\xi
is decreasing and, for any
s\in[a,b]
,

f(s)\leq\int_{s}^{b}\chi(r)f(r)\,dr+\xi(s)

is satisfied. Then, for any
t\in[a,b]
, we have

f(t)\leq\xi(t)\exp\left(\int_{t}^{b}\chi(r)\,dr\right)\,.

Proof.

This follows by standard arguments as in
[
Dra03
, Corollary 2-3]
.∎

Lemma 11.2

(A weak fundamental theorem of calculus for square roots)
.

Let
f:(0,t_{0}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
be a
C^{1}
-function. Then, we have for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
:

(11.1)

\sqrt{f(t)}\leq\sqrt{f(t_{0})}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\frac{\lvert f^{\prime}(s)\rvert}{2\sqrt{f(s)}}\,ds

Proof.

This follows from a straightforward application of the monotone convergence theorem to
g_{n}=\sqrt{f+\frac{1}{n}}
.
∎

11.1.2.
Levi-Civita tensor identites

Herein, we collect some basic identities for the Levi-Civita tensor
\bm{\varepsilon}[g]
: Firstly, it satisfies the contraction identities, where
\mathbb{I}^{a}_{b}
denotes the Kronecker-symbol:

(11.2a)

\displaystyle\bm{\varepsilon}^{ai_{1}i_{2}}\bm{\varepsilon}_{aj_{1}j_{2}}=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{I}^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}}\mathbb{I}^{i_{2}}_{j_{2}}-\mathbb{I}^{i_{1}}_{j_{2}}\mathbb{I}^{i_{2}}_{j_{1}}

(11.2b)

\displaystyle\bm{\varepsilon}^{abi}\bm{\varepsilon}_{abj_{2}}=

\displaystyle\,2\mathbb{I}^{i}_{j}

(11.2c)

\displaystyle\bm{\varepsilon}^{abc}\bm{\varepsilon}_{abc}=

\displaystyle\,6

(11.2d)

\displaystyle\nabla\bm{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\,0

The analogous formulas hold for
\bm{\varepsilon}[G]
when raising indices with regards to
G
instead of
g
.

For a tracefree and symmetric
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,2)
-tensor
\mathfrak{T}
and a
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,2)
-tensor
\mathfrak{A}
, the following simplified identites hold:

(11.3a)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}\times\mathfrak{A})_{ij}

\displaystyle={\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}}^{ab}{\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}}^{pq}\mathfrak{T}_{ap}\mathfrak{A}_{bq}+\frac{1}{3}(\mathfrak{T}\cdot\mathfrak{A})g_{ij}

(11.3b)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}\times g)_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{T}_{ij}

(11.3c)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}\times k)_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\tau}{3}\mathfrak{T}_{ij}+(\mathfrak{T}\times\hat{k})_{ij}

Further, note the following formulas (for
\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}
as
\mathfrak{T}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
as
\mathfrak{A}
and any
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,1)
-tensor
\xi
) (see
[
AM04
, p.30]
):

(11.3d)

\displaystyle\text{{div}}_{g}(\mathfrak{A}\wedge\tilde{\mathfrak{A}})

\displaystyle=-\text{{curl}}\mathfrak{A}\cdot\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}+\mathfrak{A}\cdot\text{{curl}}\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}

(11.3e)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\cdot(\xi\wedge\tilde{\mathfrak{A}})

\displaystyle=-2\xi\cdot(\mathfrak{A}\wedge\tilde{\mathfrak{A}})

(11.3f)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}\cdot(\mathfrak{A}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{T}})

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{T}\times\mathfrak{A})\cdot\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}

11.1.3.
Estimates on contracted tensors

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{T}
be traceless and symmetric
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,2)
-tensors,
\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{N}
symmetric
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,2)
-tensors and
\xi
a
\Sigma_{t}
-tangent
(0,1)
-tensor. We define
G,G^{-1}
and
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{G}
via (
2.27a
)). Then:

(11.4a)
